## Build pipeline, validator invariants, and tools

| Feature id | Behavior | Covering tests |

| CMH-VAL-23 | Declarative shadow DOM is rendered content rather than an inert template to the validator and TOC generator. A browser-eligible HTML host (the standard attachShadow host set or a valid autonomous custom element) may attach its first `<template shadowrootmode="open">` or `"closed"`; that root contributes rendered prose and headings, including a shadow-rendered report title when its outer host is top-level, while an ineligible or foreign-namespace host, a second declaration on one host, an invalid mode, or any declaration inside an outer ordinary template contributes nothing. Script/style bodies inside a rendered shadow heading remain non-visible raw text and cannot become prose or a title. The supported authoring shape is intentionally bounded: every declarative root must carry `shadowrootserializable` so file-based Save/Shareable/Offline snapshots preserve open and closed roots through `getHTML({serializableShadowRoots:true})` (a browser implementing `Element.getHTML` is required for shadow-DOM export), and a shadow host may neither be `#commentRoot` itself, mix light-DOM children with the shadow template, nor use active `<slot>` distribution (a slot parked inside a nested inert template is ignored). Document-scoped metadata, ids, content-region markers, light-DOM links/charts/mermaid blocks, and layer state inside a shadow tree stay outside the document tree and cannot satisfy document-level checks; offline security still inspects its serialized script/style/inline-style content through the template-only views. The density pass applies the same namespace and document-root boundaries. The TOC lists only the first heading per outer light-DOM shadow host and links it to that host (injecting a collision-free host id when needed), because fragment navigation cannot target an element inside either open or closed shadow roots; nested shadow headings resolve to the outer light-DOM host, shadow ids do not shift light-DOM slugs, and shadow `id="commentRoot"` / `nav.cm-toc` markup cannot establish or replace document scope. Rewriting is refused when `#commentRoot` is itself the host because the inserted light-DOM TOC would be hidden and violate the supported shape. The runtime side TOC derives a serializable closed root's label when a light-DOM heading host has no text, honoring valid-mode and first-root-only semantics. Runtime Markdown and section-review/hash walkers remain deliberately light-DOM-only because review/comment anchors do not cross a shadow boundary. | `tests/test_validate_references.py` - `test_reference_inside_a_declarative_shadow_root_warns`, `test_declarative_shadow_root_prose_is_collected`, `test_shadow_script_and_style_text_is_not_prose`, `test_an_ineligible_hosts_shadow_template_stays_inert`, `test_an_autonomous_custom_element_can_host_a_shadow_root`, `test_only_the_first_declarative_shadow_root_on_a_host_is_rendered`, `test_a_declarative_shadow_root_inside_an_inert_template_stays_inert`, `test_a_title_rendered_by_a_declarative_shadow_root_satisfies_report_kind`, `test_a_shadow_h1_under_a_top_level_host_satisfies_report_kind`, `test_shadow_raw_text_alone_does_not_satisfy_report_kind`, `test_a_declarative_shadow_root_must_be_serializable_for_exports`, `test_a_shadow_host_cannot_mix_light_dom_children`, `test_shadow_slot_distribution_is_rejected_as_unsupported`, `test_a_slot_inside_an_inert_nested_template_is_not_distribution`, `test_comment_root_cannot_itself_be_the_shadow_host`; | CMH-VAL-24 | Outside offline mode an `<iframe srcdoc>` draws an authoring-time WARNING that Export Offline removes the nested document from the frame, so an author meets that cost while they are still authoring instead of in a transient toast after the export has already emptied the frame. This is the second half of the settled `srcdoc` trade (issue #1080, recorded in CMH-OFFLINE-04): sanitizing the nested document was implemented, weighed and rejected, so the frame really does reopen empty and the only remaining gap was notice. The notice SURVIVES the recipient-side half that followed (issue #1119, CMH-OFFLINE-04): the export now keeps the nested markup beside the emptied frame as escaped inert text, so what the message says changed - the frame stops RENDERING the nested document, and the markup is preserved as text - but the notice itself does not go, because an author who needs the frame to keep rendering still has to author the snippet as content instead. Saying it was 'removed outright' after that would send them off to duplicate by hand exactly what the export already preserves. What the notice promises is bounded by what the exporter really keeps: an EMPTY or whitespace-only value keeps nothing (there is no nested document), and that case is trivially visible to this tokenizer, so it is BRANCHED rather than glossed - a gate that describes behavior the exporter does not have is the one-sided rule CMH-OFFLINE-04 exists to prevent. The exporter's other bound, a frame in a FOREIGN namespace, is invisible to a flat tokenizer with no namespace to consult, so the wording carries it instead of branching on it: what is kept is what could have RENDERED, and an `<iframe>` inside `<svg>` renders nothing. WHICH whitespace counts as empty is the literal ASCII class the exporter uses (`nested.strip(" \t\n\f\r")` against `_OFFLINE_SRCDOC_CONTENT_RE`) rather than `str.strip()`, because the two engines' defaults disagree in both directions - `str.strip()` also takes U+001C-U+001F and U+0085, JS `trim()` also takes NBSP and U+FEFF - so a `&#xFEFF;` value had this notice promise a block the exporter never inserted and a `&#28;` value had it deny one it did. PRESENCE is the test, exactly like the offline-mode error - an empty or inert `srcdoc` warns too, because the export clears those as well - so nothing here parses the nested document and the exporter and the strict gate still agree by construction. It is read off the same shared EGRESS index the offline rule uses, so a `<template>`-parked frame, a `<noscript>` fallback and a self-closed foreign element are all named exactly as the export will really empty them. It is an ADVISORY (CMH-VAL-18, prefix `offline export advisory: `), never an error and never a blocking warning: it reports what a DIFFERENT mode's export would REMOVE, so the notice never fails `--strict`, never withholds the `commentable-html-validated` stamp, and never blocks a fail-closed caller such as `retrofit`. Blocking would have made DELETING the nested document the only route to a clean handoff - the very loss the notice exists to announce. That classification answers the CONTENT-LOSS question and is deliberately NOT a ruling that a nested document is SAFE outside offline mode: no check here can see inside an attribute value, so a network load parked in a `srcdoc` escapes the self-contained errors that would refuse the same load written as an element - a gap that predates this notice and is not decided by the advisory channel. It is now CLOSED by its own rule rather than left open: CMH-VAL-25 reads the nested document as a fragment and reports the loads inside it as BLOCKING findings (issue #1125), so the two findings on one frame answer different questions - this one what an offline export would REMOVE, that one what the frame FETCHES - and a frame that reaches no network still draws this advisory alone. An offline document reports the ERROR alone, so the two halves never double-report the same frame, and the branch is deliberately "not known to be offline" rather than "known to be shareable": a MISSING or malformed layer descriptor lands here too, which is harmless because the descriptor rule already errors on it independently. The message is collapsed onto one line before truncation, because a `srcdoc` carries a whole document and is almost always multi-line while a report line is printed line-oriented (the offline ERROR shares that treatment), and it says the offline copy shows whatever local `src` the frame also carries rather than promising an empty frame - the export clears only a NETWORK `src`. | `tests/test_validate_self_contained.py` - `test_a_srcdoc_warns_that_an_offline_export_will_remove_it`, `test_the_srcdoc_offline_warning_reaches_a_parked_frame`, `test_a_frame_without_a_srcdoc_draws_no_offline_warning`, `test_an_offline_document_reports_the_srcdoc_once_as_an_error`, `test_a_srcdoc_is_not_an_error_outside_offline_mode`, `test_the_srcdoc_notice_is_an_advisory_not_a_blocking_warning`, `test_the_srcdoc_advisory_reports_on_one_line`, `test_a_srcdoc_document_still_passes_strict_and_is_still_stamped`, `test_the_srcdoc_advisory_says_the_markup_is_kept_as_inert_text`, `test_the_srcdoc_advisory_promises_nothing_kept_for_an_empty_value`, `test_the_srcdoc_emptiness_test_is_the_exporters_ascii_class_not_the_engines`; the exporter half the wording describes is pinned by `tests/49-offline-export.spec.js` - `CMH-OFFLINE-04: an iframe srcdoc carries a nested document neither the strip nor the gate can inspect`, so a reader of this row reaches the test an exporter regression would fail |
| CMH-VAL-25 | The self-contained guarantee reaches INSIDE an `<iframe srcdoc>`: outside offline mode a network reference carried in the nested document is a BLOCKING finding at the SAME severity the byte-identical reference written as an element draws - an error, except a loading `link`, which is a warning at both levels (a non-advisory warning fails `--strict` and withholds the stamp just the same). It was the one spelling that escaped (issue #1125) - every rule above reads ELEMENTS, and a `srcdoc` carries a whole document as an attribute VALUE, so the tag index reads that markup as attribute TEXT and `<iframe srcdoc="&lt;img src=https://evil.example/x.png&gt;">` validated clean while `<img src="https://evil.example/x.png">` beside it was refused. That let a shareable file carry the `commentable-html-validated` stamp and still phone home, which is precisely what the stamp tells a recipient it will not do. The nested value is read as a FRAGMENT through the SAME shared tag index (`_find_tag_attrs_egress`) every other rule here reads - the fragment lookup `_tag_attr_index` already serves for a KQL figure's inner HTML - and the SAME predicates are applied to it (`is_network_url` for `img` `src`/`srcset`, `script` `src`/`href`/`xlink:href`, `iframe` `src` and a LOADING `link` `href`; the stricter `offline_is_non_local_ref` for a nested `<base href>`, which rebases every relative reference in the nested document). So a nested element gets exactly the verdict the identical top-level element gets, and there is no second notion of what a load is. The type-keyed `src` rule CMH-VAL-08 records (issue #1144) reaches in here too: an inert data block's `src` is no more a load inside a frame than outside one, while a script a browser RUNS still is. The cheaper option the issue sketched - scanning the RAW `srcdoc` TEXT for a network-looking URL - was weighed and REJECTED: it cannot tell an `<a href>` (navigation, exempt at the top level), a `data:` URI, or a URL written in prose from a real load, so it would BLOCK benign nested markup, and a false rejection is the one failure mode a gate that withholds the validated stamp cannot afford (the over-match trade CMH-OFFLINE-05 accepts is a best-effort pattern over UNDECIDABLE scripted navigation; a fragment read here is decidable, so accepting that trade would be a choice rather than a necessity). Reading the fragment costs no two-parser drift either, which is the whole reason this is takeable where sanitization was not (CMH-OFFLINE-04): shareable mode runs NO exporter strip pass over the nested document, so this gate is the ONLY implementation of the rule and has nothing to stay in step with. It is scoped to the not-offline branch because offline mode already rejects a `srcdoc` on PRESENCE, which is strictly stronger - so the two never double-report one frame and offline behavior is unchanged. Frames are found off the same shared EGRESS index, so a `<template>`-parked frame, a `<noscript>` fallback and a self-closed foreign element are all judged alike, and a frame nested inside a frame is walked to a bounded depth. The SEVERITY mirrors the top level element for element as well: a network `link` is a WARNING there, so the nested spelling is a warning too (a non-advisory warning fails `--strict` and withholds the stamp just the same, but a plain run must not report the nested spelling of a reference more harshly than the identical top-level element). Four residuals are recorded rather than implied. (1) What it MISSES: a reference the nested document builds at RUNTIME (an inline script that assembles a URL, a nested `fetch`) - the element-level read is a fetch-shape test, not an interpreter - and the same shareable-only CSS-spelling residual CMH-VAL-08 records (a bare remote string in `image-set(...)`, a CSS escape, a URL hidden by a comment), because the nested read applies the SAME shared patterns. This residual USED to be far wider and to cost nothing, because shareable mode checked none of the media/CSS set at the top level either, so the nested read carried exactly the top-level residual rather than adding one. That is no longer so, and the nested set moved WITH the top-level one rather than lagging it (issue #1145): the two share `_MEDIA_LOAD_ATTRS` rather than restating it, so `video`/`audio`/`source`/`track`/`object`/`embed`/`input type="image"`/SVG `image`/`use`/`feImage` (including the `<image src>`/`<image srcset>` pair added in #1165, which a browser fetches because it renames the start tag to `img`) and the legacy `background` attribute are all reported inside a frame now, at the same severity their top-level twins draw, a nested `meta http-equiv=refresh` to a network target, and a nested `<script type="speculationrules">` (read on its TYPE alone, the way the top-level rule reads it, so the nested body is never parsed as JSON), and nested CSS egress in BOTH of its spellings - a `style=` attribute (through `_find_inline_styles_egress`) and a `<style>` BODY (through `_find_fragment_styles`, a scripting-disabled parse of the nested text, which is the pass that buffers style bodies at all and is also the right egress view for a `<noscript>` fallback). The `<style>` body needs that separate parse because the shared attribute index deliberately does not buffer ordinary style bodies - it would hold a second copy of the whole layer stylesheet for every cached document - and the raw-text alternative is the false-rejection trade this row rejected above; it FAILS CLOSED on its own flag rather than the attribute parse's, since it is a second, independently-failing parse and an empty style list from a parse that blew up must mean "could not look", never "this fragment has no stylesheet". The work stays bounded because a nested document is physically contained in its parent's attribute value: each distinct nested value now costs a FIXED TWO passes (the cached attribute index, plus this one uncached style pass) rather than one, and two is a constant, so a wide or deep nest is still linear in the text it actually carries. So the nested read is now NARROWER than the top-level rule in no place that the top-level rule itself is not. (2) The ONE deliberate strictness difference: a nested Chart.js CDN `src` is not exempt. The top-level exemption exists for a single documented opt-in - the loader that draws THIS document's `<canvas>` charts, whose version and SRI `check_charts` audits when the document renders one - and a copy parked inside a nested document can never be that loader, because a frame cannot draw into its host's canvas. So the exemption has nothing to exempt rather than being withheld. A network `src` on a frame that ALSO carries a `srcdoc` is reported rather than treated as shadowed, at both levels: `src` is the documented FALLBACK an engine without `srcdoc` support navigates, and Export Offline clears `srcdoc` unconditionally (CMH-OFFLINE-04), so it is exactly the reference that survives into a copy where nothing shadows it. (3) What it deliberately does NOT do: the top-level LOCAL-path `img` warning ("run tools/inline_images.py") has no nested counterpart. This is an EGRESS gate, not a portability advisor, and a relative reference in a demo frame is not a load. (4) It fails CLOSED, and both paths are pinned by mutation: a nested fragment whose parse blows up, and nesting past the depth budget, are REPORTED rather than read as "loads nothing". The walk is bounded by the DOCUMENT rather than by the branching factor - a frame's content is physically contained in its parent's attribute value and shrinks by a round of entity escaping per level, and a value is tokenized twice per batch of lookups - once for the cached attribute index and once for the uncached style-body pass, a fixed constant rather than a growing one - so a wide or deep nest costs work linear in the text it actually carries. | `tests/test_validate_self_contained.py` - | CMH-VAL-26 | The required-layer-control PRESENCE check is NAMESPACE-SCOPED, while the DUPLICATE check beside it stays namespace-BLIND and is reported INDEPENDENTLY of it, because the two questions have different answers in a browser. `getElementById` is blind, so a foreign-namespace element sharing a required id really can win the lookup and shadow the real control - that is a genuine duplicate, it is still reported off the blind `_DocParser.all_ids`, and it is reported even when EVERY carrier is foreign (the collision does not stop being one because the control is also absent). But the layer's companion UI is HTML, and the mechanism that breaks a foreign carrier is REFLECTION rather than CSS namespacing: `hidden` is an IDL attribute of `HTMLElement` alone (`SVGElement` and `MathMLElement` do not include it), so the `el.hidden = true` the layer uses to reveal and hide many of these controls (`assets/js/41-selection.js`, `43-identity.js`, `52-hover-bubble.js`, `55-toolbar-menu.js`) writes a plain JS expando on a foreign carrier, sets no content attribute, and therefore matches neither the UA rule nor the layer's OWN `.cm-skip[hidden]` author rule - which is namespace-blind, and which this same check file requires. Naming the UA rule's namespace scoping as the cause would have been unverifiable against this codebase. The reason is deliberately not narrower than that, because the ids are not all `.hidden`-driven: several are resolved by a menu-scoped `querySelector` (`assets/js/55-toolbar-menu.js`) and some are pure `textContent` sinks, so the general statement is that the control has to be the HTML element the layer's HTML-only wiring expects. Reading PRESENCE off the blind view therefore certified a document whose control the layer cannot drive: `<svg><rect id="btnAutoOpenPanel" class="cm-skip"/></svg>` in place of the real `<span>` validated with zero errors and zero warnings. Presence now reads `_DocParser.html_ids`, the same id list filtered to the HTML insertion namespace, and a required id present ONLY outside that namespace is reported as missing WITH the reason named and the carrier COUNT stated (so a two-foreign-carrier document is not told there is one), rather than as a bare absence that sends an author looking for markup that is already there. This is the element-id half of the namespace argument CMH-VAL-19 already applies to the layer views (`layer_tags`, `layer_ids`, and `#cmhAssetBanner`), and the rule applied is the BROWSER's rule per namespace rather than "reject every foreign namespace": an id at an HTML integration point (`<svg><foreignObject>`, `<math><mtext>`, and `<math><annotation-xml>` at either of the two encodings HTML5 accepts, `text/html` and `application/xhtml+xml`) still counts, because a browser really inserts that element in the HTML namespace, while the near-miss `encoding=" text/html"` - which HTML5 compares exactly, so it is NOT an integration point - does not. The integration-point tests use a `<button>` rather than a `<span>` on purpose: `span` is an HTML BREAKOUT start tag, so `<svg><span>` reaches the HTML namespace without any integration point and a `<span>` fixture would pass even with integration points disabled. The CONTENT ROOT gets the same split, and the same INDEPENDENCE, for the same reason: `_check_comment_root` reads presence off `html_ids` and duplicates off `all_ids` and reports both, because coupling them would tell an all-foreign collision only that the id collides, so removing one carrier would reveal a second error that was there all along. The runtime renders the authored document into `#commentRoot` and every export re-serializes it, which an SVGElement/MathMLElement cannot host. That half is reachable only through an INTEGRATION POINT - with a bare `<svg>`/`<math>` root the CONTENT markers stay foreign and the region check already refuses the document - but `<svg><foreignObject id="commentRoot">`, `<math><mtext id="commentRoot">` and either `annotation-xml` encoding all parsed a well-formed region and validated with zero errors and zero warnings, so the document carried the `commentable-html-validated` stamp. Only the CHECK is namespace-gated; `_record` still latches `comment_root_attrs` and `_cr_depth` on any namespace, which is safe because a foreign root either fails presence alone or, beside a real one, fails the blind duplicate first. The remaining readers of `all_ids` deliberately keep the blind view, each for its own reason: `UNIQUE_JSON_IDS` here is a duplicate-only probe; the retired-Export `FORBIDDEN_IDS` probe is a DENY detector where the broader view is the conservative one; and the `reviewedSections` probe in `layer_parts/30-state-offsets.py` is fail-CLOSED (a foreign element carrying that id still correctly draws "the id is not on a `<script>` the EMBEDDED COMMENTS region owns"). The floor this raises is honest but partial, and the limit is a NAMESPACE one: the check asks which namespace the control is in, never whether it renders. It is tag-agnostic (the shipped layer uses a `<span>` for a button, so there is no tag to require), and an HTML element that cannot be seen - parked in `<head>`, under `display:none`, or under a non-rendering `<svg><title>` - satisfies it exactly as it did before. | `tests/test_validate_layer.py` - `test_a_foreign_namespace_element_does_not_satisfy_a_required_id`, `test_a_required_id_duplicated_across_namespaces_is_still_a_duplicate`, `test_an_all_foreign_duplicate_reports_both_the_absence_and_the_collision`, `test_a_required_id_at_an_html_integration_point_still_counts`, `test_a_padded_annotation_xml_encoding_is_not_an_integration_point_for_a_required_id`, `test_a_foreign_namespace_content_root_does_not_satisfy_comment_root`, `test_a_foreign_content_root_beside_the_real_one_is_still_a_duplicate`, `test_an_all_foreign_duplicate_content_root_reports_both`, `test_duplicate_required_id_flagged`, `test_missing_required_id_auto_open_panel`; source: `tools/validate/checks/parsing.py` - `_DocParser.html_ids`, `_DocParser._record()`; `tools/validate/checks/layer_parts/00-descriptor-root.py` - `_check_element_ids()`, `_check_comment_root()` |
| CMH-VAL-27 | Outside the layer views, "will a browser execute this?" is answered by the BROWSER's rule per NAMESPACE and per script TYPE, never by tag name. `_flush_raw_captures()` used to drop the capture's insertion namespace when it finalized a `<script>`/`<style>` into `parser.scripts`, so every consumer downstream of it judged execution by the tag name plus `type` and `src` alone - the same fail-OPEN class CMH-VAL-19 closed for the layer views, still open one step further out. The finalized record now carries `ns` alongside `in_content`/`in_content_root`, and three shared predicates answer the question once for everyone who asks it. `script_code_runs(attrs, ns)` is the CODE half, and it implements HTML's "prepare the script element" rather than approximating it: an ABSENT or explicitly EMPTY `type` is a classic script; with no `type` at all a NON-EMPTY `language` makes the block type `text/<language>`, so `language="javascript"` runs and `language="vbscript"` does not; otherwise the type string is the value with leading and trailing ASCII whitespace stripped and must be an ESSENCE MATCH - an ASCII case-insensitive match for a whole JavaScript MIME type essence, or `module` - so a MIME PARAMETER defeats it (`type="text/javascript; charset=utf-8"` executes in no modern browser) and a whitespace-only `type=" "` is not a match either, because it is not "absent"; and `nomodule` returns early on the CLASSIC branch, so every module-supporting browser (which is every browser that ships) skips such a script, while a `type="module"` script carrying it still runs. The legacy `event` + `for` pair is classic-only in the same way: an element carrying BOTH is skipped unless `for` trims to `window` and `event` to `onload` or `onload()`, and omitting it left `<script src="chart.js" for="x" event="y">` accepted as a loader no browser runs. `nomodule`, `language` and `event`/`for` are all HTMLScriptElement behavior, so all three are scoped to the HTML namespace - SVG defines none of them and a browser runs an SVG script that carries one - which is the same per-namespace discipline in the other direction, and in the FALSE-REJECTION direction rather than the fail-open one. ONE boundary is deliberately left undecided: whether an SVG `<script type="module">` runs. It is treated as runnable, like an HTML one, because engine support for module scripts on `SVGScriptElement` could not be verified here and the two errors are not symmetric - calling it inert would REFUSE a document that may work, the failure mode a stamp-withholding gate must avoid most, while calling it runnable only fails to notice dead code in a shape no generator emits. The mermaid SVG control is written as a CLASSIC script for that reason, so no test asserts the unverified fact in either direction. The whitespace-only `type=" "` case is deliberate rather than an oversight and must not be "simplified" into the `type=""` branch: the algorithm tests the RAW value against the empty string BEFORE it strips, so only a literally empty value takes the classic branch. `script_code_runs` takes `ns` with an `"html"` default for the one caller whose elements are HTML by construction, and that caller passes it explicitly anyway so the reason lives at the call site; any caller iterating `parser.scripts` must pass that record's own `ns`. The fold is `_ascii_lower`, not `str.lower()`, because a Unicode fold that mapped a look-alike onto an ASCII letter would accept a type a browser never matches. `script_runs_inline_body(attrs, ns)` adds the two facts that decide whether the element's OWN child text runs: the namespace must define `script` and run it (`html`/`svg`; MathML defines none, so an element named that under `<math>` is an ordinary unknown foreign element), and the element must not load EXTERNALLY through the per-namespace source attribute (`src` on an HTML script; `href`/`xlink:href` on an SVG one, which has no `src` at all). `script_external_load(attrs, ns)` returns the URL a browser really fetches and runs, or `None`; it selects the attribute by PRESENCE with no fallback, the same test `_script_loads_externally` applies, so SVG 2's precedence of `href` over the legacy `xlink:href` is honored - a browser handed `<script href="" xlink:href="chart.js">` resolves the EMPTY `href` and never fetches `chart.js`, and falling through to the second attribute read that document as carrying a loader a browser never runs. Three consumers read these: the Chart.js LOADER search (a `<math><script src="chart.js">`, an SVG `src`, an inert or MIME-parameter `type`, or a `nomodule` tag is not a loader, so a canvas with no renderer is reported instead of validating as complete, while an SVG `<script href>`/`<script xlink:href>` to the CDN is one - and its version pin and SRI are audited like any other loader's, though the self-contained gate still REFUSES it, because the Chart.js exemption there stays deliberately bound to `src`), the INLINE chart scan (a `new Chart(` init, a `typeof Chart === "undefined"` guard, and a `getContext` draw each count only from a script whose body a browser runs), and the MERMAID loader search (a loader that never runs leaves the diagrams as source text - exactly the failure that warning names). The `_note_ready_token` gate of CMH-VAL-19 is rewired onto the same predicate, so the watchdog rule and these cannot drift apart, and it now matches the token across CHUNK boundaries with a bounded rolling tail: a browser runs the element's whole text, and this parser delivers that text in as many pieces as the source has (foreign content splits at every child element, and a CDATA section is a piece of its own), so a per-chunk match reported a watchdog that really does arm as missing. Consumers whose question is NOT execution stay deliberately namespace- and type-BLIND, and that is a decision rather than an omission: the layer descriptor and the chart-data / `handledCommentIds` / `embeddedComments` / `reviewedSections` blocks are read through `getElementById(...).textContent`, which is namespace-blind and reads any element's text, so a malformed data block under `<math>` must still be reported; the offline egress scan over `parser.scripts + parser.template_scripts` and `parser.styles` is over-inclusive on purpose, because a browser really does apply SVG CSS and template-parked content really can be adopted; and `_csp_predecessor_fetches` keeps the older, wider `_is_executable_js` because its failure direction is inverted - a predecessor it MISSED would bless a policy that really is too late, so over-counting is the safe side there. `_is_executable_js` therefore stays as the deliberately over-inclusive TYPE-ONLY test it always was (it is the validator half of the pair pinned to the exporter's `_offlineIsRunnableScriptType`), and says so in its own docstring so a later reader reaches for the execution predicate instead. | `tests/test_validate_doc_boundaries.py` - `test_every_script_and_style_record_carries_its_namespace`, `test_the_inline_body_predicate_follows_the_browser_per_namespace`, `test_the_type_test_is_a_whole_essence_match`, `test_the_legacy_event_for_pair_is_html_only_and_classic_only`, `test_the_language_fallback_is_html_only`, `test_an_empty_source_attribute_still_silences_the_inline_body`, `test_the_external_load_predicate_returns_the_url_a_browser_fetches`; | CMH-VAL-28 | The NonShareable companion lists count only a reference a browser would really USE. `_nonshareable_js_refs` filtered `_layer_tags(doc, "script")` by `src` path and extension alone, so - with the namespace half already settled by CMH-VAL-19 - a `type="application/json"` or `type="text/plain"` tag, a MIME-parameter type, or a `nomodule` one every module-supporting browser skips, still satisfied "the runtime is here" and still DECIDED the document mode, while the layer never loaded: a document with no working runtime validated clean, and a Shareable document carrying such a tag was judged by the NonShareable rule set. It now applies the shared `script_code_runs()` predicate - the same one the Chart.js loader search in `charts.py` applies to the same kind of tag - so an inert-typed or skipped companion reference reports `no commentable-html runtime` exactly as an absent one does, while an explicit JavaScript type, a legacy runnable type, an empty `type`, a `language` naming JavaScript, and a `type="module"` script (including one that also carries `nomodule`, which does nothing on the module branch) all still count. `_nonshareable_css_refs` asks the CSS half of the same question, because a `<link>` is a stylesheet only because its `rel` list says so: a `rel="preload"` / `rel="modulepreload"` / no-`rel` link pointing at the companion CSS left the layer UNSTYLED while satisfying "the stylesheet is here" and, through `_is_nonshareable`, deciding the document mode. The list is read through the shared `link_rel_tokens` tokenizer, the way every other `rel` in that file is read, so a whitespace form `str.split()` would mis-tokenize cannot smuggle one in; Two more attributes decide the same question and are tested with it: `disabled`, whose whole meaning is "not applied" and which nothing in the runtime ever enables, and `type`, since a browser obtains a stylesheet link only when that attribute is absent, empty, or a CSS MIME type essence match - so `<link rel="stylesheet" type="text/plain">` is fetched and ignored. All three are fail-CLOSED with no realistic false rejection: a document cannot disable, mistype or mis-`rel` the companion stylesheet and still expect it to style the layer. `rel="alternate stylesheet"` is deliberately left alone, because which alternate is active is a user preference this reader cannot know and no generator emits one. Narrowing these two lists narrows only the questions they answer - "is the runtime/stylesheet here", `_is_nonshareable`, and the `.assets.js` split. The STRUCTURAL checks on a reference string walk `_companion_refs`, every reference the document NAMES, because whether a baked absolute path leaks a local directory, points into a temp folder, or carries a non-file scheme is true whether or not a browser ever runs or applies the element - the disclosure is in the shipped bytes either way, and nothing else in the validator reports a local path on a `script` or `link`. Iterating the narrowed lists there dropped those reports with no second layer, so the two views are kept apart on purpose. The BOUNDARY of that is recorded rather than implied: these are NonShareable-MODE diagnostics and the whole check runs only when the document IS NonShareable, which - by this row's own rule - it is not when EVERY companion reference it names is one a browser cannot use. Such a file has no companion contract to violate; it is a Shareable document carrying a stray inert reference, and reporting it against a contract it never entered would be the very misclassification this row exists to stop. Nor does the narrowing touch the egress guarantee: a REMOTE companion URL carrying an inert type no longer draws the NonShareable "must be a local file" message, but the self-contained scan reads the shared EGRESS tag index and is deliberately type- and namespace-blind, so the same element is still refused as a network load - which message names the document changes, whether it is refused does not. | `tests/test_validate_nonshareable.py` - `test_an_inert_typed_companion_script_does_not_satisfy_the_runtime`, `test_a_runnable_typed_companion_script_still_satisfies_the_runtime`, `test_a_companion_script_type_must_be_a_whole_essence_match`, `test_an_inert_typed_companion_script_does_not_make_a_document_nonshareable`, `test_an_inert_typed_remote_companion_is_still_refused_as_a_network_load`, `test_a_companion_stylesheet_link_must_really_be_a_stylesheet`, `test_a_real_stylesheet_rel_still_satisfies_the_stylesheet`, `test_a_companion_reference_a_browser_ignores_still_reports_a_leaked_path`, `test_a_document_whose_every_companion_is_unusable_is_not_nonshareable_at_all`; source: `tools/validate/checks/resources.py` - `_nonshareable_js_refs()`, `_nonshareable_css_refs()`, `_companion_refs()`, `_is_nonshareable()`, `_check_nonshareable()` |
| CMH-VAL-29 | A CDATA section's payload is CHARACTER DATA, so it reaches every view text reaches, in every tool. `_BrowserBoundaries.parse_html_declaration` already decides WHERE `<![CDATA[ ... ]]>` is a real section (only when the current node is foreign - everywhere else it is a bogus comment ending at the first `>`, CMH-VAL-21); this settles what a real one CONTRIBUTES. A browser emits the content as character tokens and inserts them into the current node, so `<svg><script><![CDATA[...]]></script>` builds the same DOM - and runs the same script - as `<svg><script>...</script>`. The payload went to `html.parser`'s no-op `unknown_decl` and was dropped, which cost twice over: the validator reported `no bootstrap watchdog` for a NonShareable document whose watchdog really does arm (a fail-CLOSED false positive on a valid file, the mirror of the fail-OPEN cases in CMH-VAL-26), and the OFFLINE EGRESS scan - which regex-scans the same captured body - never saw a network `import()` written inside such a section, so `--strict` certified real egress as offline-clean. The handler lives on the SHARED base rather than on `_DocParser`, for the reason CMH-VAL-21 exists: the TOC generator, the section hasher, the density, contrast and deck scanners and the egress tag index all draw their element boundaries there, and an override one class lower would have let the validator collect a heading the TOC generator drops - two tools disagreeing about one document. It is routed through `handle_data` rather than into any one view, so the capture, prose, heading, mermaid-source and head-boundary views stay consistent with the browser instead of gaining a second path that drifts. Three properties are preserved rather than assumed: a section builds no COMMENT node, so a region marker written inside one is still not a boundary (which is what `marker_comment_spans` and the CMH-VAL-22 marker rule rest on); its content is RAW, so no character reference resolves in it and no `<` opens a tag; and the namespace rule of CMH-VAL-26 still gates execution, so a MathML `<script>` whose body is a section is captured but still runs nothing. | `tests/test_validate_doc_boundaries.py` - `test_a_cdata_section_payload_is_character_data`, `test_a_cdata_payload_is_not_decoded_and_holds_no_markup`, `test_a_cdata_marker_is_still_not_a_comment_boundary`, `test_a_cdata_heading_is_seen_by_the_validator_and_the_toc_generator_alike`, `test_a_cdata_payload_reaches_prose_the_way_inline_text_does`, `test_a_cdata_payload_reaches_the_section_hasher_too`; `tests/test_validate_nonshareable.py` - `test_an_svg_cdata_watchdog_still_satisfies_the_bootstrap`, `test_a_mathml_cdata_watchdog_does_not_count`, `test_a_cdata_spelling_in_an_html_script_is_just_more_script_text`; `tests/test_validate_self_contained.py` - `test_offline_egress_check_reads_a_cdata_wrapped_svg_script_body`; source: `tools/validate/checks/parsing.py` - `_BrowserBoundaries.unknown_decl()`, `_BrowserBoundaries.parse_html_declaration()` |

| CMH-BUILD-07 | Every source partial is documented in a sibling `MODULES.md` that maps it to the SPEC feature-id areas it implements, so per-module coverage is auditable. The map is an enforced gate: every partial on disk has exactly one row (no undocumented row, no stale row, and no DUPLICATE row - a repeated path is rejected on the raw rows, since the parsed map is a dict and would silently keep only the last, possibly conflicting, row), the rows are in the same directory-sort order `build.py` concatenates the partials in (a row parked out of order is how a partial gets documented twice), and every SPEC area a module claims is real and test-backed (a row for the WHOLE feature id, `AREA-NN`, exists in this spec and names a covering test - the match is anchored, so a claimed area cannot pass by being the prefix of a longer id). | `tests/test_module_coverage.py` - `test_js_modules_map_to_real_tested_spec_areas`, `test_css_modules_map_to_real_tested_spec_areas`, `test_js_modules_map_has_no_duplicate_rows`, `test_css_modules_map_has_no_duplicate_rows`, `test_js_modules_rows_follow_the_on_disk_order`, `test_css_modules_rows_follow_the_on_disk_order`; sources: `assets/js/MODULES.md`, `assets/css/MODULES.md` |

| CMH-BUILD-21 | `scripts/check_spec_test_refs.py` verifies that every commentable-html and site SPEC row points only at existing test files and that each quoted test title, class, method, or feature id resolves in that file; it additionally requires every automated test-file clause (a test-file reference followed by ` - `) to cite at least one EXACT test - a JS `test`/`it` title or a Python test method/test-case class - so a prose-only clause, a bare feature id, or a `describe` suite title alone fails the validate gate instead of drifting silently. The REVERSE direction (every test title that carries a feature id is owned and cited by that id's spec row) now covers EVERY JS `test`/`it` title in the WHOLE `*.spec.*` / `*.test.*` corpus of EVERY spec target, plain `*.spec.js` files included, not just the `*regressions*.spec.*` subset (the Python members of a corpus are still checked by the forward direction only). `FULLY_REVERSE_MAPPED_SPECS` lists all three JS targets, and a target that must start restricted registers in `INTENTIONALLY_RESTRICTED_SPECS` (the flat Python target `scripts/SPEC.md`, whose `Class.method` test names cannot carry a hyphenated feature id), so graduation is a reviewed edit rather than a silent omission; a restricted target still gets the OWNERSHIP half over its plain `*.spec.*` files, so an id no row owns can never hide there while it waits. A `describe(...)` suite title is checked for ownership too - a row cannot CITE a suite title (issue #629), but an id no row owns, parked in one, was otherwise the last carrier both directions missed. A target with NO tests directory fails closed rather than passing as a silent no-op. Because the reverse direction now demands a citation for every same-file carrier as well, the duplicate direction's same-file relaxation is no longer load-bearing; it is kept only so one miss is reported once, by the direction that explains it best. A FOURTH direction enforces the "one feature id, one behavior" premise the other three assume: a feature id may be the id cell of at most ONE feature row per spec target (of `SPEC_TARGETS` - commentable-html, the site, demo-video, and the repo-guard scripts - not every `SPEC.md` in the repo). Nothing enforced it before (issue #904) - `_spec_rows` merges same-id rows, so a test cited by EITHER row satisfied the other, and SEVEN ids owned two rows each. One enumerator (`_feature_rows`) now defines "a spec row" for BOTH enforcement and consumption, so the invariant that is checked COVERS the one that is consumed: a "Doc-surface registry" row or a row inside a fenced sample table can no longer become a coverage cell a citation is satisfied from. The relationship is a SUPERSET rather than equality - the duplicate direction is permissive on purpose (a blockquoted, two-cell, or trailing-pipe-less row still counts, so a malformed duplicate is SEEN), but only a well-formed row supplies a coverage cell, since an illustrative row quoted in prose must never own an id or satisfy a citation. The rule FAILS CLOSED: a row counts unless it sits under a `Doc-surface registry` heading AND its table header carries a `Doc surface` column (matched as a prefix against any cell, so renaming or reordering that column does not red every registered id). Keying on the header alone was the earlier version and let a duplicate be parked under any table that spelled a column `Doc surface`; excluding everything not spelled exactly `Behavior` was worse still, hiding a real duplicate behind a header the parser merely failed to recognise. The id and header cells are read as a reader sees them (wrapping emphasis, code, strikethrough, a markdown link, an HTML tag, or a zero-width character is stripped), the header is taken from the line above each delimiter row as GFM defines it so header state cannot latch past its table, and a line indented 4+ spaces is indented CODE rather than a row. Fenced code blocks are skipped per CommonMark (at most 3 spaces of indent; a backtick fence's info string may not contain a backtick; a closer must be the SAME character, at least as long, and carry no other text), so a sample table cannot trip it and a tilde line inside a backtick fence does not end it; a fence that is never CLOSED is reported and the other directions skip that spec rather than burying the cause under hundreds of "has no spec row" errors. Each spec records where a renamed id's behavior went in its "Renamed feature ids" section, since released `CHANGELOG.md` history still cites the old id. Formerly `CMH-BUILD-13` (renamed in issue #904: the duration-based shard-balancing row keeps that id, which AGENTS.md and the `00-projects.spec.js` titles mean by it, and one feature id owns exactly one spec row). | `scripts/test_check_spec_test_refs.py` - `SpecTestReferenceTests`, `SpecTestReferenceTests.test_flags_automated_clause_missing_cited_name`, | CMH-BUILD-22 | The three implementations of one contract - WHICH `(tag, attribute)` pairs a browser FETCHES when a document merely opens - are PINNED to each other by `tests/test_egress_list_parity.py`, so a widening can no longer land on one side only (issue #1179). The three are the strict layer gate (`_MEDIA_LOAD_ATTRS` plus the core `img` / `script` / `iframe` / `link` / `base` groups, the type-gated `<input type=image>` and the universal `background` attribute, CMH-VAL-08), the deck gate (`_EGRESS_ATTRS` / `_EGRESS_ANY_ATTRS`, CMH-DECK-04), and the offline export strips (CMH-OFFLINE-04). The test READS all three from their real sources - it imports the two Python modules and parses the exporter partial - rather than restating them, and asserts two relationships: the exporter strips every pair the layer gate reports (the drift direction that costs a user something, a gate rejecting the file the export just produced), and the deck gate and the layer gate carry the same pairs (outside descriptor mode `offline` the deck gate is the ONLY checker a deck gets, so a pair it lacks is a live hole). Every intentional difference is named in the test as DATA WITH A REASON and is itself checked for still being real - a stale exception fails - so nothing is silently excluded: the deck bans `iframe`/`object`/`embed` as elements and any external `<script>` outright, it reads an `<input src>` without the layer's `type=image` gate, the exporter reaches `feimage` through the case-sensitive `feImage` selector, and its non-media strips (`ping`, a form target, `srcdoc`, the speculative link rels, `referrerpolicy`, the CSS reads) are enumerated so a NEW exporter strip must be classified before it can land. The layer gate's core groups live as literals in the check body rather than in an importable list, so the test's frozen copy of them is cross-checked BEHAVIORALLY by driving the real validator once per pair, and - because a frozen table can only ever prove what it already knows - a separate direction READS the check body's own `_find_tag_attrs_egress` / `_check_network_attr` literals and refuses an unclassified rule, since a new inline rule is where every past widening landed. The exporter reading is hardened against the FALSE-PASS channels two review panels demonstrated on the first cuts. What is TEXT rather than code is excluded before anything is read: JS comments are blanked (this file's house style quotes calls and selectors inside its comments, so a strip deleted from the code but left quoted in a comment beside it was still "found", and the reading already saw one phantom selector block), and a call whose NAME sits inside a STRING literal is skipped (a `const note = 'clearAttr(el, "poster")'` would otherwise report a strip that does not exist). The comment scanner ends a quoted string at a NEWLINE as JS itself does, because without that a regex literal far above the scanned region - one carrying a quote inside a character class - opened a string state that ran for hundreds of lines and left 65 comment lines and 5 block comments standing; its tripwire checks the WHOLE file, since the scanner is stateful and an upstream desync is what reaches the region. An attribute LIST inside a delegated helper is data, not a strip: it counts only when the helper really removes through it (a loop variable handed to `removeAttribute`), so deleting that one statement takes the list's attributes with it. A UNIVERSAL `*` strip satisfies a tag-keyed pair only for an attribute whose strip really is universal (left open, any `removeAttribute` landing in the final attribute-only block blessed that attribute on every tag at once). A block's presence predicates count only when it removes the WALKED element, so an unrelated `.remove()` cannot fabricate coverage. The function bound is the MATCHING closing brace, counted past string literals, rather than the next `function` keyword; each delegate must resolve; each `all(...)` must sit at the function body's own brace depth (a call nested in a callback would take the outer block's clears); and every `all(` the depth walk sees must be one the block reader can parse, so a line-wrapped or computed selector fails loudly instead of vanishing. The deck side counts only the pairs the deck scanner can REACH: `_ActiveContentScanner._scan` filters on `_URL_ATTRS` before it consults `_EGRESS_ATTRS`, so an egress entry whose attribute is not also a URL attribute is a rule the gate declares and never applies - a one-sided landing inside a single file, which this change had to edit both lists to avoid, and which now fails with its own message. FOUR boundaries are DECLARED rather than implied, so the guard is not read as more than it is: it proves the three lists AGREE, not that they are COMPLETE against what a browser really fetches (a pair all three miss - the `<link rel=preload as=image imagesrcset>` residual CMH-VAL-08 records, closed on the offline-scoped reasoning of #999 - passes cleanly here, because completeness is a question for the threat model and for measurement, not for a parity check); it compares (tag, attribute) SETS, not the predicates each surface applies to a value (a `link` rel gate, a `base` held to the stricter non-local-ref reading - recorded in the test's shape-difference table); the two egress contracts that are NOT keyed on a pair, a `<meta http-equiv=refresh>` and the CSS `url()`/`@import` reads of #961, are outside the comparison and pinned by their own tests; and the exporter side proves a pass EXISTS for a pair, while what each strip does to a document is pinned by the offline-export Playwright suite. Every past instance of this class (#992 `feImage`, #961 the CSS patterns, #1165 `<image src>`) was found by a human or a review panel noticing a missing pair one spelling at a time; this is the mechanical version for the pair-keyed part of that contract, and it found two live divergences on the day it was written - the missing deck `feimage` rule and the deck-only `lowsrc` rule, both settled in #1179. The SVG PRESENTATION ATTRIBUTES (issue #1186) are deliberately OUTSIDE the (tag, attribute) pair comparison, for the reason the CSS reads are: the value is a CSS declaration value, so the shared `url()` reading judges it and the rule is keyed on the ATTRIBUTE alone on both sides. They are pinned instead by their own assertion, which holds the gate's measured `SVG_URL_PRESENTATION_ATTRS` and the exporter's strip list to each other AND reads the exporter's `all(...)` SELECTOR literally to check it reaches every attribute in that list - literally, because the pair reading folds a delegated helper's attributes onto every selector in its call and so cannot see a selector that lost a predicate while the helper kept it (round-1 review panel); a dropped predicate would leave an element carrying only that attribute unwalked, so the export would keep its network reference and the gate would then reject the export's own output; the deck needs no third list, since its whole-body CSS reading already covers them (CMH-DECK-04). The source reading grew with them: a universal rule written as a loop over a module constant passes a loop VARIABLE, so the variable is followed back to the `for ... in <NAME>:` that binds it and the named sequence expanded - an argument that resolves to neither a string nor a bound sequence is still reported verbatim, so an unreadable rule stays unclassified rather than vanishing. | `tests/test_egress_list_parity.py` - `EgressListParityTests`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_exporter_strips_every_pair_the_layer_gate_reports`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_deck_gate_carries_every_pair_the_layer_gate_reports`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_layer_gate_carries_every_pair_the_deck_gate_reports`, `EgressListParityTests.test_every_named_difference_is_still_real`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_exporter_reading_is_not_silently_empty`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_exporter_reading_names_every_strip_it_finds`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_comment_stripper_removes_prose_without_eating_code`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_comment_stripper_leaves_no_comment_in_the_real_file`, `EgressListParityTests.test_a_quoted_call_inside_a_string_is_not_a_strip`, `EgressListParityTests.test_a_delegated_attribute_list_counts_only_with_a_real_removal`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_decks_own_two_lists_agree`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_deck_reachability_filter_still_matches_the_deck_scanner`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_layer_gates_source_names_no_unclassified_rule`, `EgressListParityTests.test_the_frozen_core_groups_are_really_enforced`, and `EgressListParityTests.test_the_presentation_url_attribute_lists_agree` (the attribute-keyed #1186 contract, read from both real sources) |
| CMH-BUILD-23 | No name is declared more than ONCE in the runtime bundle's shared scope. The `assets/js/NN-*.js` partials are concatenated into one classic script inside one IIFE, so every partial's top-level `function` / `class` / `const` / `let` / `var` binding shares a single scope. A redeclared `function` is LEGAL there and the LATER declaration silently wins, so a duplicate survives the whole suite as dead code (issue #1183 shipped two identical `_cmhCommentableLink` declarations that way, baked into `SHAREABLE.html` and every built example); a duplicate `const` / `let` / `class` is the opposite failure - a bundle-wide SyntaxError that renders every report as inert HTML, reported against a line in a multi-megabyte artifact rather than against the identifier. A delimiter-aware walk (strings, template literals with `${}` nesting, line, block and Annex B HTML-like comments, regex literals, ASI, and the brace/paren/bracket stack) collects every shared-scope binding from BOTH the concatenated partials and the BUILT layer, and names the identifier, its kind, and every partial and line that declares it. The walk FAILS CLOSED rather than guessing: a destructuring binding (whose names it does not unpack), an identifier escape, a mismatched closer, and a delimiter stack that unwinds out of the shared scope are each REPORTED, not skipped. Its result is then cross-checked against a REAL JS engine: where `node` is on PATH (CI always has it) the bundle body is run as a script whose first statement is a `throw`, so V8 instantiates the scope - creating every top-level function binding - before executing a single statement of the runtime, and every name V8 hoists must be one the walk found; V8 must also PARSE the body, which is what independently settles the duplicate `const`/`let`/`class` half, since that is a SyntaxError. Two engine-free cross-checks back it up: the stack must come back empty and the shared scope must be the bundle's outer wrapper (asserted structurally, not by byte offset), and a plain COLUMN-0 regex must not find any name the walk already knows declared MORE often than the walk did - which is #1183's exact shape, both copies at column 0. So an under-count of a column-0 declaration reds the gate instead of quietly narrowing it, and the controls make a walk that stopped reporting fail rather than pass vacuously. The walk is a heuristic, not a parser, and the layering above is what bounds it rather than any claim to see every binding; the DOCUMENTED exclusion is HOISTING out of a nested BLOCK - a `var` written inside a block or a `for` header, and an Annex B `function` declared inside a block (this bundle is sloppy mode), both bind in the same function scope but are not tracked, because telling a block brace from a function-body brace is the cost and the runtime already has ~100 nested `var`s. A duplicate SHARED-SCOPE `var` is refused as a policy, since a second initializer silently overwrites the first and two partials claiming one name is an accident either way. | `tests/test_vendored_libs.py` - `test_no_declaration_in_the_bundle_is_made_twice`; source: `assets/js/*.js`, `skill/dist/commentable-html.js` |
| CMH-BUILD-24 | The tutorial capture's mermaid route is VERSION-AGNOSTIC about the URL and STRICT about the bytes, so a mermaid bump can neither break screenshot capture nor silently render it from the wrong release. `tools/capture_tutorial.mjs` aborts every remote fetch to stay hermetic and registers one narrower route that serves mermaid's version-pinned jsDelivr import from the local `node_modules/mermaid/dist`. That route is the only thing standing between the capture and the catch-all abort, and the URL it must match is single-sourced from `package.json` and restamped into the shell on every build - so a route that pinned a LITERAL version (`mermaid@11.16.0`) stopped matching the moment the dependency moved, mermaid never loaded, and `npm run shots` failed on a `waitForMermaid` timeout that pointed at nothing. The route therefore matches any version on that CDN path and stays narrow otherwise (a different package, or the same path on a different host, is still aborted). Being version-agnostic about the URL would on its own trade a loud failure for a SILENT one - the local entry module's relative chunk imports resolve back through the same route, so the render is internally consistent at whatever version happens to be on disk, and the committed PNGs would be rendered from a mermaid that never ships - so `assertInstalledMermaidIsPinned` refuses to capture at all when the installed mermaid differs from the `package.json` pin, naming the fix. The refusal DECISION is extracted into `tools/mermaid_pin.mjs` so it is exercised directly rather than asserted about as source text: agreement proceeds, a STALE install is told to run `npm ci`, and a lockfile that has moved ABOVE the pin is told to bump the pin and re-vendor instead (advising `npm ci` there would loop, since `npm ci` reproduces it). What the tests below prove is the route's PATTERN (version-agnostic, still narrow), that `routeVendoredMermaid` really CALLS the guard, and each branch of the refusal decision; the handler's fail-closed behavior - aborting rather than reaching the network when the vendored file is missing, and the `startsWith(dist + path.sep)` traversal guard - is UNCHANGED by this row and is deliberately NOT claimed as covered here: the heavy capture spec exercises the capture end to end on the happy path only, so the missing-file abort and the traversal guard have no direct test and are named as a coverage gap rather than asserted. | `tests/test_capture_mermaid_route.py` - `test_the_capture_route_matches_the_current_pinned_mermaid_cdn_url`, `test_the_capture_route_does_not_pin_a_literal_mermaid_version`, `test_the_route_still_only_matches_mermaid_on_the_pinned_cdn`, `test_the_capture_refuses_to_render_with_a_mermaid_that_is_not_the_pinned_one`; `tests/01-vendor-provenance.spec.js` - `the mermaid pin-agreement decision covers match, stale install and lockfile-ahead (CMH-BUILD-24)`; `tests/54-tutorial-shots.spec.js` (end-to-end capture); source: `tools/capture_tutorial.mjs` - `routeVendoredMermaid`, `assertInstalledMermaidIsPinned`, `tools/mermaid_pin.mjs` |
| CMH-BUILD-25 | The vendored MERMAID bytes are pinned to the version the repo DECLARES, and to the tarball npm verified. `assets/vendor/mermaid.min.js` is hand-copied and is the copy an `Export Offline` artifact inlines, so it is the only mermaid a reader of an offline document ever runs - yet nothing else in the build reads its CONTENT (`build.py` stamps the ONLINE pin from `package.json`, and its `--check` only proves the committed `.gz` decompresses to the committed `.js`, `CMH-BUILD-11`). A vendored file left at the old version, or fetched from somewhere other than the locked tarball, would therefore pass every gate while the shipped offline bytes disagreed with the version the changelog, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` and the online pin all advertise. `chart.umd.min.js` has no equivalent binding yet, so this row is deliberately scoped to mermaid. Two layers close that: the bundle must self-report the version `package.json` pins and `UPSTREAM.md` must name it (no install, no network, so it holds everywhere), and - where `npm ci` has run, which is the `fast` CI job - the installed version must EQUAL the pin (`npm ci` installs the LOCKFILE version, and a lockfile that has drifted above the pin is itself the defect, so that is a FAILURE rather than a skip: skipping there would go quiet in exactly the lockfile-only-bump case the guard exists for) and the vendored file must be BYTE-IDENTICAL to `node_modules/mermaid/dist/mermaid.min.js`, extending npm's integrity-hash verification of the tarball to the shipped bytes. The BUNDLED DOMPurify version is recorded too, because upstream mermaid prebuilds its dependencies into that file: a `dompurify` bump in `package-lock.json` does NOT reach these bytes (`mermaid@11.16.1` inlines DOMPurify `3.4.0`), and reading such a bump as a fix for a bundled-DOMPurify advisory is exactly the mistake this pins against. The MIT licence that must travel with the redistributed bytes (`CMH-LICENSE-01`) is held to the same standard: presence is not enough, since a STALE notice still contains the word MIT, so the vendored `.LICENSE` is compared BYTE-FOR-BYTE with the one in the installed tarball alongside the bundle. | `tests/test_vendored_mermaid_provenance.py` - `test_the_bundle_reports_the_version_package_json_pins`, `test_upstream_md_names_the_version_that_is_actually_vendored`, `test_upstream_md_records_the_dompurify_version_the_bundle_really_inlines`, `test_the_mit_licence_travels_with_the_vendored_bytes`; `tests/01-vendor-provenance.spec.js` - `the vendored mermaid bundle is the npm tarball the lockfile pins (CMH-BUILD-25)` (bundle AND licence byte-identity); source: `assets/vendor/UPSTREAM.md`, `assets/vendor/mermaid.min.js` |
| CMH-BUILD-12 | The Playwright suite is split into two projects (`playwright.config.js`) so CI runs them as independent parallel jobs: `heavy` is exactly the browser-subprocess specs listed in `tests/_projects.mjs` (`HEAVY_SPEC_FILES`, e.g. the tutorial-screenshot capture spec), run in its own sharded job with a bounded worker count so its concurrent capture subprocesses do not thrash a shard; `fast` is every other spec, sharded across runners at the default worker count. The two projects are exact complements (`testMatch`/`testIgnore` over the same globs), so every spec runs in exactly one project - a guard test asserts each `HEAVY_SPEC_FILES` name resolves to a real, unique spec file so a typo can never make the heavy job silently run nothing while that spec thrashes in `fast`. | `tests/00-projects.spec.js` - `every heavy-project spec name resolves to a real, unique spec file (CMH-BUILD-12)`; sources: `plugins/commentable-html/dev/playwright.config.js`, `tests/_projects.mjs`; CI: `.github/workflows/plugin-tests.yml` runs the `fast` and `heavy` projects as separate sharded jobs |
| CMH-BUILD-13 | The `fast` Playwright job is sharded by DURATION, not by Playwright's count-based `--shard`: the CI job passes `PLAYWRIGHT_FAST_SHARD=i/N` and `playwright.config.js` restricts the `fast` project to the specs `tests/_shard.mjs` assigns to shard i by longest-processing-time (LPT) bin-packing over committed per-spec timings (`tests/spec-timings.json`), so every shard's total run time stays within ~1% and no single shard is the bottleneck. Balance is self-maintaining: `npm run shard:timings` regenerates the timings and the assignment rebalances. A guard test asserts the timings list exactly the fast specs (no spec missing a timing, no orphan entry for a deleted spec) and that, for every plausible shard count, the LPT partition covers each fast spec exactly once and the worst shard stays within 1.5x of an even split (a spec too slow to fit its fair share trips this, signalling it should be split). | `tests/00-projects.spec.js` - `fast-project spec timings are complete and shard-balanced (CMH-BUILD-13)`, `LPT shard assignment is deterministic and lossless (CMH-BUILD-13)`; sources: `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tests/_shard.mjs`, `tests/spec-timings.json`, `playwright.config.js`; CI: `.github/workflows/plugin-tests.yml` `playwright` job sets `PLAYWRIGHT_FAST_SHARD` |
| CMH-BUILD-14 | The shared `startStaticServer` Playwright test helper (`tests/helpers.js`) closes DETERMINISTICALLY. Node's `server.close()` resolves only once every connection has ended, and a browser holds HTTP keep-alive and speculative-preconnect sockets open after loading a document and its companion assets, so a bare `server.close()` intermittently hangs waiting for an idle socket to drain and stalls a spec's `finally { await server.close() }` teardown until the whole test times out (issue #677, the Export Offline nonshareable region-guard spec, which opens four keep-alive sockets: the HTML plus its `.css`/`.js`/`.assets.js` companions). The helper destroys open connections on close (`server.closeAllConnections`, Node >=18.2) so close() always resolves promptly. | `tests/52-static-server-close.spec.js` - `startStaticServer close resolves promptly with an idle client socket open, not hanging teardown (CMH-BUILD-14)`; source: `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tests/helpers.js` (`startStaticServer`) |

The sidebar "Generated on" line therefore reports when the demo was built and is IDENTICAL across every `examples/report-*.html` and `examples/deck-showcase.html`, instead of an authored in-story date (the taxi report's `2014-12-31`), a drifted per-file date, or no date at all; an authored `data-generated` in a `dev/examples/src/` source is overridden and one is added where absent. | CMH-BUILD-16 | The tutorial screenshots are rendered and verified by ONE renderer on BOTH sides - the digest-pinned `mcr.microsoft.com/playwright` container - so the two environments cannot drift apart. Every shots npm script (`shots`, `shots:check`, `shots:digest`) routes through `tools/shots_linux.py`, the short habitual command is the safe one, and extra `capture_tutorial.mjs` arguments ([example] [outDir] [prefix], `--print-paths`) pass straight through (with `allow_abbrev=False`, so a future capture flag that merely prefixes a wrapper flag cannot be swallowed) so even a single-scene recapture stays on the guarded path. The raw path is closed rather than merely discouraged: `capture_tutorial.mjs` REFUSES to render or verify the COMMITTED assets dir unless a renderer marker in the environment says the wrapper invoked it, so `node tools/capture_tutorial.mjs` cannot rewrite the committed PNGs with the host's fonts (capturing into any other directory, which the suite does, is unaffected). The container renders on EVERY host (Windows, macOS, Linux) and in CI alike: the required `playwright-heavy` job validates the committed PNGs by running the tool itself with `--check` - deliberately NOT the `--skip-without-renderer` npm script, so the gate cannot skip itself whatever the runner environment - and pulls the image in a separate timed step (`--print-image`, so the pull can never warm a different image than the render uses); a test asserts those once-only steps share one `matrix.shard` condition whose value the matrix actually produces, so the gate cannot become unreachable. There is no host-platform fast path to get wrong - `host_matches_ci_renderer()` is gone (a test asserts it cannot come back), and because the runner's own Ubuntu release no longer decides a pixel the job tracks `ubuntu-latest` again with no runner/variant coupling to maintain. The image is pinned on both axes that decide the pixels - the `@playwright/test` version resolved in `package-lock.json` (the chromium binary) and the Ubuntu release in the image variant (the fonts) - plus `--platform linux/amd64` so an Apple Silicon host does not render arm64, `--ipc=host` and a writable `HOME` so a `/dev/shm` exhaustion or a passwd-less uid cannot crash the render, `--user` on a LINUX host only (macOS has `os.getuid` but no matching passwd entry in the image, and Docker Desktop already maps ownership) so no root-owned PNGs are left in the worktree, and `CI=true` forwarded in CI so the capture keeps the doubled settle deadlines docker would otherwise drop. Nothing hardcodes the tag. The pin is by DIGEST, not just by tag: `tools/shots-image.lock` records the sha256 the tag resolved to (rewritten by `npm run shots:digest`), so a registry rebuild of `v<ver>-noble` on a newer base OS cannot silently change the renderer; a lock that does not cover the resolved version degrades to the mutable tag with a loud note naming the re-pin command on a developer machine (a Playwright bump never blocks a local regeneration) but is FATAL in CI, which refuses to validate against whatever the registry serves that day, and it reds a test until it is re-recorded; only a well-formed sha256 STRING for the pinned repository is ever used, and a wrongly typed lock value degrades to that same path instead of raising. Docker is required by the shots commands only, never by development or the suites: `shots:check` (which `npm test` runs) SKIPS with a note when Docker is missing or its daemon is down (a wedged daemon probe times out rather than hanging), but NEVER in CI, and the write direction never skips anywhere. `--native` renders with the host browser as an explicit, non-authoritative escape hatch that warns on every run and that no npm script uses. Each precondition failure exits non-zero with a distinct, actionable message (Docker not installed, naming the image and the way out; daemon down; `node_modules` missing, pointing at `scripts/setup_dev.py` and worded for the native or container path). `scripts/rebuild_all.py` drives the same wrapper - needing Docker and the mounted `node_modules`, not a host `node` - so it regenerates the shots correctly on any host with Docker and skips only that step when the pinned renderer cannot run. | `tests/test_shots_linux.py` -
