| CMH-BUILD-17 | Element-clipped tutorial screenshots are DIMENSION-STABLE across renderers. Their clip height is derived from the element's laid-out height, which depends on FONT METRICS, not on the browser. The capture does not use the report's own font stack: `freezeMotion` pins it to `font-family: Arial, sans-serif !important`, and that pin is only as shareable as Arial is - Windows resolves real Arial, while the pinned Linux container ships no Arial at all and fontconfig substitutes Liberation Sans, which is metric-compatible with Arial in ADVANCE WIDTHS (so the width is identical to the pixel and no shot drifts horizontally) but not in VERTICAL metrics. Issue #698 measured `.cmh-checklist` under identical CSS (`font-size: 15px`, `line-height: 23.25px`) and an identical lockfile-pinned chromium: each row renders 23.391 CSS px tall on Linux versus 23.250 on Windows, so the element measures 230 CSS px instead of 228, which at `deviceScaleFactor: 2` is a 4 device px PNG delta with no content change. `tools/shot_clip.mjs` is the single source of that geometry (`CLIP_QUANTUM`, `DEVICE_SCALE`, `DIMENSION_DELTA_PX`, `MAX_WIDTH_DELTA`, `quantizeClipHeight()`, `clampedClipHeight()`, `heightDeltaAllowed()`), and every CONTENT-derived clip height in `capture_tutorial.mjs` - the element clip, the fixed-region clip, and the comment-search clip - is snapped onto that grid, so the drift collapses onto one value and the PNG dimensions stop depending on which renderer produced them (verified: the same checklist scene renders 2444x464 on a Windows host and in the pinned container). A clip bounded by the viewport or by a panel is quantized LAST and DOWNWARD (`clampedClipHeight`), so a clamped clip lands on the grid too; quantizing up and then clamping would leave an off-grid height, and an element that clamps on one renderer but not the other would differ by an arbitrary sub-quantum amount - the same bug moved rather than fixed. Only the height is quantized, because the layout width comes from the fixed viewport; full-viewport clips are a fixed 1320x900 box and are left alone. The guarantee holds while the per-shot renderer drift stays under one quantum (about 0.6% of a clip height for the measured font pair, so ~5.4 px at the 900 px viewport ceiling); a wider gap moves two quanta and fails loudly, which is the signal to raise the quantum rather than widen the budget. Because no pure function of a drifting measurement can be boundary-free, the `--check` HEIGHT comparison (`heightDeltaAllowed`) admits exactly one non-zero value - one whole quantum - and only BETWEEN TWO HEIGHTS THAT ARE BOTH ON THE GRID: an exact value rather than a band keeps a sub-quantum delta failing (that can only be real content added or removed at the bottom edge, which the overlap-cropped pixel diff cannot see), and the on-grid requirement keeps the allowance away from clips that are NOT quantized, so removing or appending exactly one quantum of visible rows on a fixed-viewport shot still fails. Width keeps its own strict budget. The comparison lives once in `tools/shot_compare.mjs` - dimension gate in Node, only the pixel diff in the browser - imported by both the tool and the heavy freshness spec (they previously carried byte-identical copies that could silently diverge), and `--print-paths` reports the geometry and budgets so the two cannot drift apart. | `tests/80-shot-clip.spec.js` - `quantizeClipHeight collapses any sub-quantum drift onto the grid (CMH-BUILD-17)`, `quantizeClipHeight never crops the element and is idempotent (CMH-BUILD-17)`, `a clamped clip lands on the grid and never exceeds its bound (CMH-BUILD-17)`, `the straddle allowance applies only between two on-grid heights (CMH-BUILD-17)`, `dimensionsMatch keeps width strict and rejects an unreadable PNG (CMH-BUILD-17)`, `capture_tutorial reports the shared clip geometry and budgets it renders with (CMH-BUILD-17)`, `every committed tutorial shot is on the clip grid or full-viewport (CMH-BUILD-17)`; `tests/54-tutorial-shots.spec.js` - `a grid-line straddle in the baseline does not fail --check, a smaller shift does (CMH-BUILD-17)`, `regenerates every garden shot into a nested out dir, and --check passes on fresh output (CMH-TUT-SHOTS-01)`; source: `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tools/shot_clip.mjs`, `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tools/shot_compare.mjs`, `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tools/capture_tutorial.mjs`; docs: `docs/testing-guidelines.md` |
| CMH-BUILD-18 | A failed screenshot drift gate leaves the pixels it saw behind as evidence. Because the tutorial PNGs are rendered ONLY in the pinned container (CMH-BUILD-16), a contributor without Docker cannot reproduce a drift failure locally, so the CI run has to carry the proof: `capture_tutorial.mjs --check` KEEPS its freshly rendered PNGs in `tmp/tutorial-shots-check/<pid>` on ANY unsuccessful run - a stale or missing shot, and equally an exception thrown mid-capture (a browser crash, a selector timeout), which previously fell through the cleanup and DELETED the shots already rendered - and deletes them only on a clean pass, and the required `playwright-heavy` job uploads that tree as the `tutorial-shots-drift` artifact instead of letting it die with the runner (unzipped, its root is the `<pid>/<scene>/` tree). For each FAILING shot the check also writes a `<scene>-<name>.diff.png` beside the fresh PNG, painting every pixel that differs by more than the gate's own channel tolerance in magenta over a faded copy of the committed shot. It renders on the MAXIMUM bounds of the pair and marks any pixel present in only one image, so a dimension change - a normal failing result - is marked rather than clipped away into a diff with no magenta at all, so a reviewer sees WHAT moved instead of eyeballing two near-identical screenshots. It is produced only on failure (so a green run pays no extra render), lands inside the tree the artifact already uploads (so no workflow change is needed), and is best-effort - a diff that cannot be rendered is skipped and never changes the pass/fail outcome. The upload is conditioned on the gate STEP's own outcome (`failure() && steps.shots.outcome == 'failure'`), not the job's, so a green run never pays for it and the shards that skip the once-only gate never upload an empty artifact; a gate that failed before the comparison (an unpinned digest, a docker error) warns rather than adding a second, misleading failure, and `overwrite: true` keeps "Re-run failed jobs" - which reuses the run id an artifact name is unique within - from dying on the existing artifact instead of recording the fresh evidence (one name is enough because only the single once-only shard ever uploads). The evidence is kept for 14 days: long enough to outlive a review round without paying the default 90 days of storage for a failure that is usually fixed the same day. The action is pinned by full commit SHA like every other third-party action, and `docs/testing-guidelines.md` names the artifact and the committed files to compare it against, so a rename cannot silently orphan the instructions. | `tests/test_shots_linux.py` - `DriftEvidenceRetentionTests` (`test_the_scratch_is_removed_only_on_a_clean_pass`, `test_the_clean_flag_is_set_only_after_the_comparison_passed`, `test_a_failure_says_where_the_evidence_is`), `MidCaptureCrashTests` (`test_a_crash_after_the_first_scene_keeps_the_rendered_pngs` drives the REAL check through the pinned renderer with the `CMH_SHOTS_FAIL_AFTER_SCENE` fault hook and asserts the already-rendered PNGs survive, `test_the_fault_hook_is_unset_in_a_normal_run`), `DiffImageTests` (`test_the_comparator_exports_a_diff_writer`, `test_the_check_writes_a_diff_only_for_a_failing_shot`, `test_the_diff_lands_inside_the_uploaded_scratch_tree`, `test_a_diff_failure_cannot_change_the_gate_outcome`); `tests/81-shot-diff-image.spec.js` - `a differing pair produces a diff image marking the changed pixels`, `an identical pair marks nothing`, `a missing input is reported rather than throwing`, `a dimension change is marked, not silently dropped`, `the diff marks exactly what the gate counts, at the tolerance boundary`, `the tolerance the diff marks with is the same one the gate counts with`; `tests/test_shots_linux.py` - `test_a_drift_failure_uploads_the_fresh_screenshots`, `test_the_evidence_upload_never_runs_on_a_green_job`, `test_the_artifact_action_is_pinned_by_commit_sha`, `test_the_guide_tells_a_contributor_where_the_artifact_is`; source: `.github/workflows/plugin-tests.yml` - the `playwright-heavy` drift-evidence upload, `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tools/capture_tutorial.mjs` - `checkScreenshots` keeps the scratch on failure; docs: `docs/testing-guidelines.md` |
| CMH-BUILD-19 | The tutorial-screenshot drift gate is EXACT: no differing pixels are allowed, sized to the determinism ONE pinned renderer actually gives rather than to two renderers agreeing. It used to downsample 2x, quantize colors onto a 64-step ladder (snapping near-white to white) and tolerate a channel delta of 96 across up to 20% of the pixels, so a real visual regression could pass the required gate unseen - a budget sized before the renderer became a single digest-pinned container. Issue #710 measured what that renderer gives: two independent renders of all 19 committed shots are BYTE-IDENTICAL, in the container and on a host browser alike, including under four concurrent captures and a 6-worker stress run. So `tools/shot_compare.mjs` applies NO normalization (no downsample, no quantize, no near-white snap - `PNG_DOWNSAMPLE` and `PNG_QUANTIZE_STEP` are gone) and `MAX_DIFF_PIXELS` is 0. The volatile BUILD STAMPS that would otherwise repaint a shot on every release - the runtime version badge and the document's "Generated on" date (which falls back to `document.lastModified`, volatile per checkout) - are neutralized at CAPTURE time (`tools/shot_stamps.mjs` rewrites them to the fixed `v1.x` / `Jan 1, 2026` placeholders before each clip is measured and again before each shot is taken) instead of being bought off with a pixel allowance: an allowance applies to the WHOLE image, so a budget wide enough for a repainted badge is also wide enough to hide a small real regression anywhere, and because the same comparison drives the WRITE path, `npm run shots` would refuse to update a shot whose intended change fell inside it. The freeze is deliberately NARROW - only the elements the runtime stamps those two values into (`#cmVersion`, `.cm-version`, `.cm-footer-ver`, the Help heading and About paragraph; `#cmGenerated`, `.cm-footer-gen`) and only the value inside them - so layer copy that merely LOOKS like a stamp (a rendered comment) is never rewritten and the authored document (`#commentRoot`) is never touched, even when it names the same version or date. Each label is preserved and a value that is not a real rendered date (`unknown`, a malformed stamp) is left alone, so a vanished badge, a reworded label or a broken stamp still moves the shot and fails; only the date VALUE is deliberately un-gated by screenshots. The freeze is idempotent, so a shot cannot depend on how often it ran. The one deliberate slack is `PIXEL_CHANNEL_TOLERANCE` 2: nothing measured needs it, it is insurance for the single input the digest pin does NOT fix (the host CPU, since Chromium rasterizes in software and Skia dispatches on CPU features), it is invisible at 2 steps out of 255, and the smallest regression it still catches is a delta-3 wash where the retired budget waved through delta 96. The rule is a pure function (`withinPixelBudget`) in Node, so only the pixel COUNT is computed in the browser, and `compareImages` reports WHAT differed - a dimension mismatch, or the differing-pixel count and ratio against the budget - so a red `--check` run or determinism assertion names the magnitude instead of only failing, and a jitter budget can be re-sized from a measurement rather than a guess. `--print-paths` reports the budget and the placeholders so the capture tool, the `--check` gate and the specs that mirror it cannot drift apart; `tests/54-tutorial-shots.spec.js` imports the same comparator rather than copying it, so it can never be stricter or laxer than the gate (it shares the comparator, not the renderer - it captures with the runner's own browser and compares two runs in the same environment). | `tests/80-shot-clip.spec.js` - `the pixel diff is exact and rejects a deliberately altered shot (CMH-BUILD-19)`, `no differing pixel is allowed, whatever the shot size (CMH-BUILD-19)`, `the budget is exact, its tolerance is invisible, and the tool reports both (CMH-BUILD-19)`, `the capture freezes the volatile build stamps, and only in the review layer (CMH-BUILD-19)`; `tests/54-tutorial-shots.spec.js` - `garden capture is deterministic across two independent runs (CMH-TUT-SHOTS-01)`; source: `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tools/shot_compare.mjs`, `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tools/shot_stamps.mjs`, `plugins/commentable-html/dev/tools/capture_tutorial.mjs` - the `--print-paths` budget report and the freeze call sites; docs: `docs/testing-guidelines.md` |
| CMH-BUILD-02 | `dev/VERSION` is the single source of truth for the version; `build.py` reads it and stamps the runtime `CMH_VERSION` const, `pkg/plugin.json`, the marketplace entry, and each document's `<meta name="commentable-html-version">`. Companion filenames are version-agnostic, so a version bump does not rename dist files. `dist/manifest.json` records SHA-256 and byte size per companion and is serialized with sorted keys for a deterministic, diff-stable payload. | `tests/test_build.py` - version single sourced, manifest hashes, `test_manifest_is_deterministically_key_sorted`, single declaration tests |

| CMH-BUILD-04 | Generated fixtures and examples are kept fresh against current assets and exercise the feature surface. Each shipped `examples/report-*.html` is a pure build artifact assembled by `build.py` from its INDEPENDENT content source in `dev/examples/src/report-*.html`: `build.py` swaps in the current CSS/COMMENT UI/JS layer and re-stamps the `<meta>` version, and `--check` compares the shipped file to a fresh assembly, so a hand-edit or a stale/clobbered committed example - of its CONTENT as well as its layer - fails the build (closing the self-sourced hole the site pages had before #91; content edits must be made in `dev/examples/src/`). `build.py --check --check-fixtures` additionally runs the Playwright fixtures' `generate.mjs --check` (skipped only when node is absent), so the required `dist-in-sync` gate also owns fixture freshness (a version bump that leaves a fixture stale fails the fast gate, not only the slow `plugin-tests` job). | `tests/10-more.spec.js` - `committed sample fixtures are not stale (generate.mjs --check)`; `tests/test_examples.py` - example exists, validates, inlines images, exercises every feature, `test_examples_embed_current_version`, `test_build_check_catches_example_drift`, `test_build_check_flags_an_orphaned_example_with_no_source`; `tests/test_build.py` - `test_check_subprocess_passes`, `test_check_fixtures_passes_and_is_reported` |

| CMH-BUILD-11 | The vendored rich-libraries blob (the inline gzip+base64 mermaid/Chart.js payload) is reproducible across zlib implementations. `build.py` does NOT recompress the libraries at build time (gzip DEFLATE output differs between stock zlib and zlib-ng, which Python 3.14 ships, so a live recompress makes the dist blob depend on the builder's machine and breaks `dist-in-sync`); it reads a committed `assets/vendor/<lib>.gz` artifact and only base64-encodes it, which is deterministic everywhere. `build.py --regen-vendor-gz` regenerates those `.gz` artifacts when a vendored library changes (the only step that gzips), and `build.py --check` fails when a committed `.gz` is missing, corrupt, or does not DECOMPRESS to the current source (decompression is deterministic across zlib impls, so the guard never recompresses). | `tests/test_vendored_gzip.py` - `VendoredGzipReproTests.test_blob_reads_committed_gz_not_recompressed`, `test_drift_guard_detects_missing_and_stale_gz`, `test_regen_roundtrips_through_decompression` |
| CMH-BUILD-26 | The layer that SHIPS carries no developer commentary. `build.py` runs the source through `tools/build_parts/05-minify.py` after the version stamp and before every consumer, so `dist/commentable-html.js`, `dist/commentable-html.css`, the asset registry, both templates and every built example embed one identical stripped copy; `assets/js/**` and `assets/css/**` stay fully commented and remain the single source of truth. Measured on #1250's corpus the commentary was roughly 44% of a 1.2 MB runtime and rode along in every generated document and every offline export. The transform is deliberately NOT a general minifier - no identifier mangling, no statement joining, no semicolon insertion - it only DELETES comments and layout whitespace, and never a byte inside a string, template literal or regex literal. Five properties make it safe to run unattended: (1) ASI is preserved exactly - wherever the source had a LINE TERMINATOR (any of LF, CR, U+2028, U+2029, not LF alone) the output has one, and a block comment that CONTAINS one collapses to a newline rather than a space, because the spec treats such a comment as a line terminator; (2) whether a `/` opens a regex is DECIDED FROM PARSE CONTEXT, never guessed, because a wrong guess silently rewrites the regex (`if (ok) /\d+ \w+/.test(s)` shipping as `/\d+\w+/`) or swallows a comment opener and deletes the rest of the file: after `]`, an identifier, a number, a string, a template literal or a trailing `.` (a numeric literal may end in one, as in `1. / 2`) an expression has ENDED so `/` divides; after `)` it opens a regex only when that paren closed an `if`/`for`/`while`/`with` HEADER; after `}` only when that brace closed a BLOCK rather than an object literal or an arrow body, where a `function`/`class` body counts as a block exactly when the keyword stood in STATEMENT position (`function f(){}` may be followed by a regex, `const f = function(){} / 2` divides); and a regex keyword only opens regex context when it is a keyword rather than a property name (`x.return / 2` divides). A regex literal is then copied through verbatim, and the separator after it is never dropped (`/re/ instanceof X` must not close up into `/re/instanceof`, where the flags would swallow the operator); (3) an inter-token space is removed only when the two characters cannot fuse - identifier pairs, an identifier before a `\uXXXX` escape, `1 .toFixed()`, `+ +`, `/ /`, `/ *`, `= "`, and the markup delimiters `< !`, `< /` and `- >` all keep their separator, so the strip can never manufacture an HTML attribute or close the `<script>` element the layer is inlined in; (4) a source that leaves a comment, string, template literal or template SUBSTITUTION unterminated is refused, so a truncated file cannot be silently shipped as whatever part of it was still valid; (5) the result is checked twice - the strip re-reads its own output and compares literals and whitespace-free code (which catches an assembly that dropped or invented a byte, but shares a scanner with what it checks and so is NOT a defence against a misread - property 2 is), and `verify_js_syntax` parses the stripped bytes with an INDEPENDENT parser (`node --check`) whenever node is present. Legal-notice comments (`/*!`, `@license`, `@preserve`) are kept, which is how the vendored lz-string MIT notice - copyright and permission text in full - keeps travelling inside every document and offline export: unlike mermaid and Chart.js, whose notices the offline exporter emits beside the inlined library, lz-string is baked into the runtime, so that comment is the only notice accompanying the redistributed copy. CSS keeps every inter-token space (descendant combinator, `calc()`) and never ADDS one: a CSS comment is removed at tokenization and leaves no whitespace token, so `.a/**/.b` is the compound selector `.a.b` - but it does TERMINATE the token before it, so where the neighbours would fuse (`bl/**/ue`, `1px/**/2px`) the comment is kept as an empty `/**/`. | `tests/test_minify.py` - `JsCommentStripTests`, `JsSeparatorSafetyTests`, `AmbiguousSlashTests`, `UnterminatedSourceTests`, `LineTerminatorTests`, `LicenseCommentTests`, `RoundTripGuardTests`, `SyntaxOracleTests`, `CssCommentStripTests`, `ShippedLayerTests`; and the whole Playwright suite, which runs against the stripped built stage |
| CMH-BUILD-27 | A per-component byte budget gates the shipped payload. `tools/size-budget.json` names a ceiling for each generated component that documents embed verbatim (the runtime, the stylesheet, the asset registry and both templates); `build.py` measures them on every build AND every `--check`, prints each one against its ceiling, and REFUSES - before it writes anything - when one is exceeded, so the required `dist-in-sync` job fails a pull request that blows a budget and no oversize artifact is left in the tree for the next `git add` to sweep up. A budgeted component the build no longer produces is also a failure, so renaming an output cannot silently retire its ceiling; so is a generated `dist/` PAYLOAD component (a script, a stylesheet or a template, as opposed to metadata such as `manifest.json`) that no ceiling covers, so the payload cannot simply move into a new unbudgeted file; and a missing or malformed budget file is a build failure rather than a silent skip. Raising a ceiling is a deliberate, reviewed edit to that file plus a changelog line: #1250 measured the runtime growing 3.5x across versions with nothing to notice it, because no single commit looked expensive. Two limits are stated in the file so a reader does not over-trust it: it is a CEILING, not a ratchet (growth under the headroom is invisible, and a shrink does not tighten it automatically, because a true ratchet would make every unrelated feature PR edit it), and it budgets the shipped LAYER, which a document embeds verbatim, not the document - the vendored diagram payload and a document's own generated DOM are workstreams A and D of #1250 and have no ceiling here. | `tests/test_minify.py` - `SizeBudgetTests` (`test_the_committed_budget_covers_the_generated_components`, `test_the_built_stage_is_inside_its_budget`, `test_an_oversize_component_fails`, `test_a_component_inside_its_budget_passes`, `test_a_budgeted_component_the_build_no_longer_produces_fails`, `test_a_missing_or_malformed_budget_is_a_build_failure`, `test_the_budget_file_documents_why_it_exists`); `tests/test_build.py` - `BuildTests.test_main_check_ok_prints_version`, `BuildTests.test_main_writes_outputs_removes_stale_and_reports_sizes` |
