| CMH-VAL-11 | The validator warns when a `<pre><code class="language-XXX">` block declares a HIGHLIGHTABLE language (one `tools/blocks/highlight_code.py` supports, resolving its aliases like `cs`->`csharp`) but carries no `cmh-code-*` token spans, i.e. it was authored with a language label but never run through the highlighter, so it renders as monochrome text. Only BLOCK code (a `<code>` inside a `<pre>`) is checked; an inline `<code class="language-...">` in prose, a non-highlightable label (`language-text`, `language-kusto`, ...), and an empty block are all skipped, and an already-highlighted block passes. The single source of truth for "is this highlightable" is `highlight_code.LANGUAGE_CONFIGS`/`ALIASES`. SCAN BOUNDARY: blocks are LOCATED as PARSED ELEMENTS by the shared tokenizer `checks/parsing.code_block_spans()` (which the KQL runnable scan, CMH-KQL-08, also uses), never by matching text over the document, because a `<pre>`/`<code>` mentioned in layer CSS or JS prose otherwise starts a greedy match that SWALLOWS the author's real block and silences the whole check. That is not a corner case: a Shareable document inlines exactly that prose, so the guardrail was blind for every Shareable document, and a raw block quoted inside a script body or a comment is data, not authored content, so it is never flagged. Parsing draws the boundary a BROWSER draws, which closes four blind spots a text scan had: (1) EVERY HTML raw-text / RCDATA element holds text, not markup - `script`, `style`, `textarea`, `title`, `xmp`, `iframe`, `noembed`, `noframes` and `noscript` (raw text in a scripting-ENABLED browser, the only mode a commentable document runs in) - so a block quoted in one contributes nothing and its unpaired opener cannot swallow a later real block; (2) `<![CDATA[ ... ]]>` is a CDATA section only when the CURRENT NODE is a foreign (`svg`/`math`) element, which is the rule a browser's tokenizer applies: under an HTML INTEGRATION POINT (`foreignObject`, `desc`, `title`, or `annotation-xml` whose `encoding` is `text/html`/`application/xhtml+xml`, plus the MathML text integration points `mi`/`mo`/`mn`/`ms`/`mtext`) the children are HTML again, and an HTML BREAKOUT start tag (`p`, `div`, `pre`, `code`, `table`, ... ) pops the open foreign elements - so in either case `<![CDATA[` is a bogus comment ending at the very first `>` and live markup after that `>` is still inspected, while a genuine section inside foreign content is not mistaken for markup; (3) the legacy comment close `--!>` (and the abrupt `<!-->` / `<!--->`) really ends a comment, so markup after it stays live instead of being blanked to the document's next `-->` - which the layer always supplies; and (4) a `>` inside a QUOTED attribute value no longer truncates a tag, so `<code title="a > b" class="language-python">` is still recognized. Because the HOST interpreter's own rules differ (html.parser knows only `script`/`style` as raw text before Python 3.13, closes a comment on `--\s*>` but not on `--!>`, stops a raw-text region only at the canonical `</script>`, and resumes tokenizing after the next `>` in an unterminated comment), every boundary is applied EXPLICITLY - the raw-text set and its SCAN boundary via `set_cdata_mode`, the raw-text closer (`</script data-x>`, `</script/>`, whose own quoted attribute values cannot end it early), the comment closes (and NOT `-- >`), an unterminated comment running to the end of the document, and the CDATA rule - so the same document validates identically on every interpreter the skill runs on. Those boundaries are not private to this scan: they live in the shared `_BrowserBoundaries` base the document parser derives from as well (CMH-VAL-21), so the `<pre>`/`<code>` view and the chart / link / id view of one document can never disagree about where an element ends. NOTHING is raw text inside foreign content: HTML5's "in foreign content" insertion mode takes a `<script>` or `<style>` start tag through "any other start tag", inserting a foreign element and leaving the TOKENIZER in the data state, so an SVG `<title>`, `<script>` or `<style>` body holds MARKUP a browser builds and is parsed rather than swallowed. The tokenizer only LOCATES spans - the language, the emptiness test and the highlight classification all read the ORIGINAL bytes at those offsets, so a block whose inner is a raw `<script>` is inspected rather than looking empty. Its records are FROZEN (the result is cached and shared between the two checks, so a consumer that stamped a field on a record - or on its nested attribute mapping - would silently poison the other check's view). SCOPE LIMIT: only THIS document's own elements are scanned. Markup written inside an ATTRIBUTE VALUE that a browser parses as a separate nested document (`<iframe srcdoc="...">`) is not an element of this document, is never something the highlighter could have baked, and is deliberately not inspected; the text scan reported it only as a side effect of matching raw text. SEVERITY SPLIT: an unhighlighted block is FATAL (the author fixes it by baking, and a fail-closed caller must refuse to write it). The "carries markup the highlighter did not emit" finding is an ADVISORY carrying `HIGHLIGHT_ADVISORY_PREFIX` only when the leftover markup is INERT, which is decided by a WHOLE-TAG allowlist: every `<` in the block's inner must open a well-formed open/close tag of a plain inline formatting element (`span`, `mark`, `b`, `em`, `code`, `br`, ...) carrying at most a quoted `class`. Text BETWEEN those tags is escaped source and is never inspected, so a code sample that merely mentions `onclick=` or `javascript:` stays advisory. Anything else - an unrecognized tag, ANY other attribute (`style`, `is`, `data-*`, an event handler), an unquoted or malformed attribute, a stray `<` - is NOT inert and stays FATAL, because a `<pre>` body is parsed as markup and that content executes, loads, or navigates; that is an escaping bug, the same line CMH-DIFF draws for `<pre class="cmh-diff">`. Matching whole tags rather than scanning for dangerous attributes is what makes the rule safe: an attribute scanner has to model quoting to know where a tag ends, and any mistake there hides the NEXT tag (`<b x=1'><script>...` slipped a live script past an attribute-scanning version). The rule is deliberately conservative in the SAFE direction - a false "not inert" only keeps the pre-existing fatal behavior, while a false "inert" would let executable markup pass `--strict` and be stamped. FAIL CLOSED ON A DESTROYED STRUCTURE: a raw `<script>`, `<style>` or `<!--` opened INSIDE a code block and closed outside it swallows the intervening `</code></pre>`, so the block would vanish from the scan - and the browser's raw-text mode swallows `</code></pre>` the same way, so the script RUNS. The tokenizer reports any `<pre>` or `<code>` that never got its OWN end tag - at either level, including one closed only implicitly by an ancestor's end tag or left open at end of document - and the check turns that into a FATAL warning instead of silently inspecting nothing. It also reports a parse that FAILED outright, in which case it hands back NO blocks at all (a partial list would let a check conclude the rest of the document is clean) and both this check and CMH-KQL-08 refuse the document rather than reporting it clean. | `tests/test_validate_highlighting.py` - `test_unhighlighted_language_code_block_warns`, `test_unhighlighted_alias_language_code_block_warns`, `test_highlighted_code_block_is_clean`, `test_non_highlightable_language_code_block_is_clean`, `test_code_block_without_language_is_not_flagged`, `test_inline_language_code_is_not_flagged`, `test_layer_css_and_js_prose_does_not_blind_the_check`, `test_a_code_block_quoted_inside_a_script_body_is_not_flagged`, `test_a_code_block_quoted_inside_an_html_comment_is_not_flagged`, `test_a_script_named_inside_a_comment_does_not_mask_the_real_block`, `test_a_comment_opened_inside_a_script_body_does_not_mask_the_real_block`, `test_a_script_is_not_closed_by_a_style_closer`, `test_a_raw_text_closer_carrying_attributes_still_ends_the_mask`, `test_a_mask_delimiter_inside_a_tag_attribute_opens_no_mask`, `test_a_pre_swallowed_by_a_raw_script_fails_closed`, `test_the_block_payload_is_read_from_the_original_document`, `test_hand_written_markup_in_a_code_block_is_an_advisory`, `test_the_advisory_wording_asks_for_no_remediation`, `test_non_inert_markup_in_a_code_block_stays_fatal`, `test_escaped_code_text_mentioning_a_handler_stays_advisory`, `test_the_not_highlighted_warning_stays_fatal`, `test_a_raw_text_element_body_cannot_swallow_the_real_block`, `test_cdata_in_foreign_content_cannot_swallow_the_real_block`, `test_the_legacy_comment_close_ends_the_comment`, `test_a_gt_inside_a_quoted_attribute_does_not_truncate_the_tag`; | CMH-VAL-15 | The validator emits a non-fatal WARNING when a `report` or `plan` section is a wall of prose: a run of `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_LONG` (4) or more consecutive long paragraphs (past a tunable `MIN_LONG_PARAGRAPH_CHARS` length) inside `#commentRoot` with no table, list, figure, diff, chart, or diagram breaking it up, nudging the author toward a real skimmable layout. It uses a dedicated stack-based HTMLParser density pass (not the single-pass token stream), is scoped to `#commentRoot`, ignores `cm-skip` subtrees and paragraphs nested inside layout blocks (an INLINE `cm-skip` inside a paragraph excludes only its own text and does not split it), treats an inert `<template>` as invisible - a browser renders none of a `<template>`'s contents, so paragraphs, headings, layout blocks, a kind meta, and end tags parked inside one contribute nothing, the element itself never breaks a run, an end tag inside the fragment can never close an element opened outside it, and a template left unclosed at end of input (or a self-closed `<template/>`, which HTML5 opens like any non-void tag) keeps the rest of the input inert - with two exceptions a browser DOES render: a `<template shadowrootmode="open"/"closed">` declarative shadow root is read as an ordinary transparent container (matched as the ENUMERATED attribute it is, so `" open"` is not `open`, and only the FIRST such template on a given host attaches - a second sibling stays inert, as does one parked inside another template - and a kind meta inside the shadow tree does not set the document kind, since a browser renders a shadow tree but never applies its metadata to the document), and a `<template>` written inside raw-text or RCDATA content (`<title>`, `<textarea>`, `<noscript>`, `<script>` ...) is prose a reader sees, not a tag, so it cannot open the fragment. The pass draws its element boundaries from the shared browser base rather than the host, so the raw-text set, the `</name` + whitespace/`/`/`>` closer, namespace stack, foreign self-closing rule, HTML non-void self-closing rule, and end-of-input rules are identical on every interpreter (CMH-VAL-21); `<noscript>` is raw text because a commentable document only ever runs with scripting enabled, while an SVG `<title>` or `<script>` remains parsed markup and a self-closed foreign `<template/>` never opens an inert HTML fragment. It tracks sections as a heading stack so nested and headless sections are labeled by their own heading and each distinct wall (a run of consecutive long paragraphs) is reported - two walls in the same section separated by a layout block or a nested section are both reported, anchors the `<section>` run-break to a genuinely matched frame (a stray `</section>` never suppresses a wall), resets the run at any layout block / heading / `<section>` boundary (and at a `cm-skip` block), and runs ONLY for `report` and `plan` (`slides`, `board`, `generic`, and a missing or unknown kind do not emit this advisory). | CMH-VAL-16 | The validator ERRORS in NonShareable mode when a BAKED absolute or `file://` companion reference resolves inside an OS temporary directory that is not the document's own folder, because the OS reaps temp directories and the shared document silently loses its whole layer. Temp detection is cross-platform: the machine's `TMPDIR`/`TEMP`/`TMP` and `tempfile.gettempdir()` roots (symlink-resolved via `realpath`, so macOS `/var`->`/private/var` and Windows 8.3 short names do not diverge) plus root-ANCHORED path fragments (`/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `/var/folders`, `/windows/temp`, and the per-user `AppData/Local/Temp`), so a durable project folder literally named `tmp` is never mis-flagged. Relative refs bake no absolute location and are never flagged (so the temp-dir-based hermetic tests are unaffected), and a companion sitting BESIDE the document keeps its prior behavior (absolute-path warning / clean `file://`). | CMH-VAL-17 | The validator's single-pass script scanner (`_js_scan`) models REGEX LITERALS, so a `/.../` containing a backtick, a single quote, or a double quote cannot flip the scanner into a phantom string/template state that blanks the rest of the script and hides real code from every check that reads it (this bit for real: a Markdown fence regex containing a backtick made the shipped showcase deck fail E3 with "no renderer was found" because the chart's `.getContext(` had been blanked). A `/` inside a `[...]` character class does not terminate the literal, escapes are honored, and every JS line terminator (LF, CR, U+2028, U+2029) ends a literal candidate - escaped or not - and a `//` comment; a comment also separates identifier tokens (`else/*c*/if` is not `elseif`). Division is told apart from a literal by a prev-significant-TOKEN heuristic: a closed string / template / regex literal, an identifier, a number, a postfix `++`/`--`, or a closing bracket means DIVISION, while an operator, the start of the body, a control-head `)` (`if (x) /re/.test(s)`, including `else if` and `for await`), or a RESERVED word (`return`, `typeof`, `case`, `default`, `extends`, ... - never a contextual word that can be a variable name such as `of`, `yield`, or `await`, and never a same-named property such as `obj.return`) opens a literal. Ambiguity and a literal that does not terminate on its line both resolve to DIVISION, so mis-reading a division as a literal never blanks live code, and a quote left open by such a read is not treated as a string opener (a quoted string may not span a line, CRLF continuations included), which contains the damage to that line; a regex missed right after a `}` (a statement block and an object literal are indistinguishable here) whose body holds a backtick is the accepted residual. The regex lookahead is BUDGETED per script body, so a pathological line of unterminated candidates cannot make the single pass quadratic. The literal is blanked in BOTH views, so a `new Chart(` or a `typeof Chart === "undefined"` that appears only inside a regex counts as neither init nor a guard. | `tests/test_validate_charts.py` - `ChartValidatorTests.test_regex_literal_backtick_does_not_blank_the_script`, `test_regex_literal_quote_does_not_hide_init`, `test_regex_character_class_slash_does_not_end_the_literal`, `test_division_is_not_read_as_a_regex_literal`, `test_new_chart_inside_a_regex_literal_is_not_init`, `test_division_after_a_closed_literal_is_not_a_regex`, `test_postfix_and_property_keyword_divisions_are_not_regexes`, `test_regex_after_a_control_head_paren_is_a_literal`, `test_regex_after_an_else_if_control_head_is_a_literal`, `test_regex_after_a_for_await_control_head_is_a_literal`, `test_regex_after_export_default_is_a_literal`, `test_contextual_keyword_named_variable_divides`, `test_separated_plus_signs_are_not_a_postfix_operator`, `test_line_comment_ends_at_any_js_line_terminator`, `test_unclosed_quote_is_contained_to_its_own_line`, `test_unterminated_regex_candidates_do_not_blow_up`, `test_regex_after_extends_is_a_literal`, `test_escaped_line_terminator_does_not_extend_a_regex_candidate`, `test_comment_separates_identifier_tokens`, `test_regex_shaped_guard_text_is_not_a_real_guard`; source: `tools/validate/checks/parsing.py` - `_js_scan()`, `_regex_can_start()`, `_scan_regex()`, `_string_closes_on_its_line()` |
| CMH-VAL-18 | Advisory validator warnings are classified in ONE place and honoured consistently by every caller. `validate.ADVISORY_PREFIXES` lists the stable prefixes of findings the author cannot clear - or must not be FORCED to clear (two today: `code highlighting advisory: `, the deliberately hand-written code block of CMH-VAL-11, and `offline export advisory: `, the CMH-VAL-24 notice that Export Offline would remove an `<iframe srcdoc>`, which reports what a DIFFERENT mode's export would REMOVE - blocking on it would make deleting the nested document the only route to a clean run, which is the very loss the notice exists to announce) -, and `validate.is_advisory()` / `validate.partition_warnings()` split a warning list into (fatal, advisory). An advisory is always REPORTED, but it never fails `validate.py --strict`, `finalize.py --strict` or `upgrade.py --strict`, never blocks a fail-closed caller (`retrofit.py`, `content_replace.py`, `chart_block.py`), and never withholds the `commentable-html-validated` stamp - blocking on a finding the author cannot (or must not) clear would leave such a document permanently unstampable with the runtime "not validated" banner up. Every other warning stays fatal, so the fail-closed guarantee is unchanged for actionable findings; in particular the theme-contrast advisory (CMH-THEME-02) is deliberately NOT in the set, because its near-miss band ships a concrete `--suggest` fix and is therefore clearable - `retrofit.py` composes its own long-standing carve-out for it on top of `ADVISORY_PREFIXES`, and fails CLOSED (every warning fatal) if `validate` cannot be imported rather than keeping a second copy of the prefixes that could drift. | `tests/test_validate_cli.py` - `test_advisory_only_document_passes_strict_and_is_stamped`, `test_a_fatal_warning_fails_strict_and_is_not_stamped`; | CMH-VAL-19 | The NonShareable determination, and every check that follows from it, read the LAYER's own markup rather than the whole document: the authored CONTENT region is excluded, because the layer's companion references always sit OUTSIDE it - the CSS `<link>` in `<head>` before it, the runtime `<script>`s at the end of `<body>` after it. So a document that legitimately DEMONSTRATES the companion markup in its prose (a doc about commentable-html, or a Shareable file migrated by `to_shareable.py`, which anchors its rewrites to the same region) is no longer misclassified as NonShareable and no longer fails with `mode must be "nonshareable"` / missing runtime / missing `#cmhAssetBanner` / companion-not-found. The region is taken from the PARSE (`_DocParser.layer_tags` / `layer_ids` / `layer_ready_token`, gated on the existing `_in_commentable_content()`), never from marker offsets in the text, so it is exactly the region a browser would agree on: real HTML comment markers, inside the live `#commentRoot`, outside an inert `<template>`, and never inside CDATA. That closes the ways a text-offset view could be steered into blanking the LAYER instead - a BEGIN marker moved ahead of the real stylesheet, an END marker moved past the real scripts, or a `<style>`/`<script>` straddling a marker so the closing tag falls inside the blanked span and the rest of the document parses as CDATA - each of which would have reported a broken NonShareable document as Shareable. It holds in the other direction too: an authored demonstration can never STAND IN for a real reference, so a NonShareable document whose companion reference, `#cmhAssetBanner`, bootstrap watchdog token, or version meta exists only inside its prose still reports exactly the error it always did, and companion markup inside a `<template>` (inert, never loaded) is ignored. Because the layer view is only as trustworthy as the region it is derived from, the layer check also ERRORS when the CONTENT markers are present in the TEXT exactly once each and in order but the document does not PARSE with a well-formed region - opened by a real BEGIN comment inside the live `#commentRoot` and closed again by a real END comment. A marker swallowed by a `<script>`/`<style>` body or an inert `<template>`, a marker outside `#commentRoot`, or unbalanced markup closing `#commentRoot` mid-region would otherwise silently misattribute the markup after the broken boundary; refusing is the only safe verdict. The LAYER DESCRIPTOR is resolved through the same kind of boundary, for the same reason and in the same DIRECTION the runtime and the exporter already enforce it: `cmhLayerBlocks` drops any owner the content ROOT contains ("a content-region decoy must not be able to declare what this document IS") and every export refuses a document whose only owner sits there, so a validator that took the first `<script id="commentableHtmlLayer">` anywhere in the parse certified a file the layer resolves no descriptor for and no export will produce - and, since the DECLARED mode became the sole key for the offline rule set, let authored content pick the rule set that judges it. A descriptor inside the content root therefore does not count: a document whose ONLY descriptor sits there is reported as MISSING one (with the reason named, as the runtime and the exporter name it), and a decoy placed there ahead of the real descriptor never decides the mode. The boundary is the whole `#commentRoot` SUBTREE, not just the part between the CONTENT markers, because `cmhLayerBlocks` tests `root.contains(node)`: a descriptor parked inside the root but ahead of the BEGIN marker (or past the END marker) is equally invisible to the layer, so narrowing to the marker region would have left that half of the divergence open. The parse carries both flags (`_DocParser` keeps each `<script>`/`<style>` capture's `in_content` and `in_content_root` when it flushes it), and the root subtree CONTAINS the authored region, so the authored-DEMONSTRATION case above is covered by the same test - a document that quotes the descriptor markup in its prose while carrying the real one in the head still reads the real one. The layer views are NAMESPACE-AWARE for the same reason: an element merely NAMED after an HTML one is not that element, and filing it by TAG NAME alone let a bootstrap watchdog, a companion `<link>` or a `<script src>` written inside `<math>` satisfy a check a browser never honors - the runtime never boots and the layer never loads - with no `<template>` involved. The insertion namespace rides along into `_DocParser._record()` and onto every `<script>`/`<style>` capture, and the rule applied is the BROWSER's rule PER NAMESPACE rather than "reject every foreign namespace": `layer_tags` and `layer_ids` take HTML-namespace elements only (a browser loads a stylesheet only from an HTML `<link>`, honors `src` only on an HTML `<script>` - an SVG script loads from `href`/`xlink:href` and MathML defines no `script` at all - and the runtime reveals `#cmhAssetBanner` through `.hidden`, an HTMLElement property whose UA `[hidden]` rule is namespace-scoped, so a foreign element carrying that id is found by `getElementById` and can then never be shown or hidden), while `layer_ready_token` is still accepted from an SVG `<script>`, because a browser really does run an inline one. The token must also come from a script whose INLINE BODY runs: a browser that fetches an external script ignores the element's own child text, so a token folded into the companion `<script src>` tag - or into an SVG `<script href>`/`<script xlink:href>`, since an SVG script has no `src` at all - never arms the watchdog and no longer satisfies it. `all_ids` stays namespace-blind, because `getElementById` itself is. | `tests/test_validate_nonshareable.py` - `test_a_mathml_script_does_not_satisfy_the_bootstrap_watchdog`, `test_an_svg_script_still_satisfies_the_bootstrap_watchdog`, | CMH-VAL-20 | A check about the LAYER is never answerable by text an AUTHOR or a REVIEWER supplied. Each of the three asks its question of the right SOURCE rather than of the document string. (a) The two CSS questions - is the scoped `.cm-skip[hidden]` rule present, and is a `--cp-bg:` theme variable declared - plus the unscoped-`[hidden]` warning read real parsed `<style>` BODIES reduced to the part that can be a selector or a declaration, because text is not a stylesheet, a commented-out declaration is not a declaration, and a string VALUE is neither - live CSS such as `content: ".cm-skip[hidden] --cp-bg: x"` declares nothing. That reduction is a small LEXER, not a regex, because every mistake it avoids is reachable: `/*` and `*/` inside a quoted string are ordinary characters (a regex would blank the LIVE declarations between them), an unterminated comment runs to end of input in a real parser (a regex would leave its text able to satisfy a check), and a string ENDS at a raw newline (a bad-string token), so a stray quote cannot swallow the rest of the stylesheet. Each `<style>` is lexed INDEPENDENTLY, as a browser parses it, so an unterminated comment in one cannot hide a later element's live unscoped reset; a `<style>` whose `type` is neither absent, empty, nor `text/css` renders nothing and is dropped. Reading document text let authored prose, a reviewer's embedded comment, or even a layer `<script>` that merely MENTIONS `--cp-bg:` satisfy them, so a document with no theme variables at all passed the ERROR by talking about them; conversely a `<pre>` code sample SHOWING the unscoped rule raised a warning its author could only clear by rewording their content. Every parsed `<style>` counts wherever it sits - one an author puts in their own content is still live CSS - so the narrowing is by CSS-ness, not by region, and an unclosed `<style>` (which a browser runs to EOF) is flushed by `_DocParser.close()` rather than escaping the scan. (b) The retired `--START-COMMENTS-EXPORT--` marker, which is about markup, is probed with an ALLOW-list: `layer_regions_text()` returns only the CSS, COMMENT UI and JS regions. A deny-list cannot be made safe, because user text reaches `<title>`, `data-doc-label`, `<meta>` and every attribute - `new_document --label` copies the label verbatim into two of them - and the HANDLED IDS / EMBEDDED COMMENTS regions exist precisely to CARRY user text (comment bodies, reviewed-section headings an export bakes in), so those two are state containers and are never inspected. The structural detector remains `FORBIDDEN_IDS` over the parsed DOM. (c) The CONTENT and region markers are counted AND located in one shared `content_marker_scan()` view whose `<script>`/`<style>` bodies are blanked while comments survive (the markers ARE comments), so `_check_content_markers` and the layer views can never disagree about what a marker is, and a marker quoted in script data neither forges a duplicate-marker ERROR nor defines a boundary. That scan takes its raw-text boundaries from the same TOKENIZER the code-block spans use (CMH-VAL-19), so a `<script` NAMED INSIDE a comment never opens a region that swallows a marker, a `>` in a quoted attribute value never ends a start tag, and an unclosed `<script>` runs to end of document exactly as a browser runs it. A marker is also a comment with PROVENANCE, not merely a comment NODE whose text reads like one: a browser synthesizes a comment from `<!BEGIN: commentable-html - CONTENT ...>`, `<?...>` or `</` + junk, and the count views here match the EXACT source the authoring tools emit, so the parse view opens or closes the CONTENT region only on a comment whose own source IS that marker (CMH-VAL-21). Without that, a forged declaration - or a real but uncounted `<!--BEGIN: ...-->` / `<!-- BEGIN: ... --!>` - inside `#commentRoot` opened the region while the document's counted `BEGIN` marker sat outside it, the one place the parse-derived check exists to refuse, and the document validated clean. A marker is also a boundary only where a browser PARSES it. Text has no notion of an inert `<template>` (whose content does not run, does not load, and which `getElementById` never sees), of a CDATA section in foreign content (character data, not markup), or of a RAW-TEXT body (`<script>`, `<style>`, `<textarea>`, `<title>`, `<noscript>`, ...; text a reader sees), so the count view counts a marker written in any of them while no live parse builds a comment node there - a document could satisfy the layer's region check with a marker no live parse sees, and every parse-driven check keyed on that region then failed OPEN. The chart-init guard was where that bit: `check_charts` SKIPS E5 when no `END: commentable-html - JS` comment was parsed, deliberately, so that a plain chart page which is not a commentable-html document at all is not flagged (CMH-CHART-02) - so a `new Chart(` before such a marker was not reported and the whole document validated with zero errors and zero warnings. The parse therefore records the SPANS of every comment that mentions the marker text, split by whether it is inside a `<template>`, and `check_layer` CROSS-CHECKS the markers the region count ACCEPTED - the same matches, over the same RAW document, so nothing the count accepted can escape the cross-check - against them: a counted marker no parsed comment covers is refused, and the diagnostic names the template, the raw-text body, or neither. The test is "is there a real comment here", not "is the comment source byte-canonical", because the shipped documents write their BEGIN markers inside a decorated comment (a rule of `=` lines plus prose) the count view reads a marker line out of - demanding a canonical source would refuse every real document. The one sanctioned non-comment marker is the Shareable CSS region's, a `/* ... */` comment inside a LIVE `<style>` that a browser never turns into a comment NODE, so it is matched against that `<style>` BODY (by tag AND by not being parked in a `<template>` - a dead stylesheet may not host the region either, or a whole inert CSS region validated clean and `upgrade.py` then wrote its new stylesheet into the fragment; and NonShareable's CSS markers, real HTML comments there, stay covered by the comment rule). The check also runs in the OTHER direction: blanking a raw-text body deletes whatever closed an open comment state in the raw view, so the blanked view can count a bare marker line the raw view rejects - a marker no cross-check over the raw count could see, and one that silently empties the layer-region ALLOW-list (`layer_regions_text`, which counts in that view) - so a blanked-view count HIGHER than the raw count is refused (lower is the CSS region, whose markers that view blanks). If the raw-text pass fails while the document parser succeeds the two views cannot be reconciled at all, and the document is refused rather than accepted unchecked. The JS END marker carries one extra rule, because it is the only counted marker a check reads a POSITION from: `js_end_marker_pos` accepts a NARROWER comment source than the count view does, so a comment carrying decoration or prose around the marker, or closed with the legacy `--!>`, satisfied the count while leaving the guard's boundary unset - the two must now name the SAME comment or the document is refused. A `<template>` that carries no region marker is untouched. | `tests/test_validate_layer.py` - `test_export_marker_inside_authored_content_is_not_flagged`, `test_export_marker_warns`,
