| CMH-VAL-22 | Every implementation that locates a region marker IN TEXT answers one canonical corpus identically. The rule lives in four places that cannot share a helper - the runtime (`_cmhRegionMarkerMatches`, inlined into every document), the validator package, the shipped authoring tools and the maintainer-only build tool are separate distributions - so `tests/fixtures/region_marker_parity.json` is the contract instead: `tests/test_region_marker_parity.py` pins the three Python copies and `tests/59-region-marker-parity.spec.js` pins the runtime, over the same cases (inline `<!-- -->` and `/* */` forms, a bare marker line inside a block comment, `=` decoration, tab padding, CRLF, prose that is not a marker, a name carrying regex metacharacters, and one case per separator below). A LINE breaks on `\n` ONLY, the way the runtime's raw-text scan sees it: Python's `str.splitlines()` also breaks on `\x0b`, `\x0c`, `\x1c`, `\x1d`, `\x1e`, `\x85`, `\u2028` and `\u2029`, and treats a lone `\r` as a terminator, so a marker "line" that exists only after one of those splits used to be COUNTED by the validator, the build and the upgrade tool while the runtime that reads the file back ignored it - two views disagreeing about which comment IS the boundary, the same class of defect the count views were aligned on in CMH-VAL-20/21. `validate.py` and `upgrade.py` read with `newline=""`, so their CLI paths preserve that raw line model instead of letting Python turn a lone `\r` into `\n`. The validator separately normalizes CRLF and lone CR to LF for every BROWSER-SEMANTIC check, matching the HTML input stream; before using that normalized view it compares each relevant marker count with the raw view and refuses any marker that exists only because a lone CR was normalized. The upgrader's raw offset table advances on `\n` only, retaining CRLF width without inventing a line at a lone CR. The three Python copies also expose the same `_MarkerMatch.start(group)` / `end(group)` signature, and REFUSE any group but 0 or 1 (which are deliberately the same span) rather than answering a wrong one, so a caller written against `re.Match` fails loudly. One bound is recorded rather than papered over: the corpus stays inside the Basic Multilingual Plane, because JavaScript indexes UTF-16 code units and Python indexes code points, so an astral character would make one document resolve to two different numbers with both sides right; a test pins that. The four copies are LINE LOCATORS, not boundary deciders, and that is the recorded answer to whether a marker quoted in raw text may be counted: they all still FIND a marker-shaped line inside a `<script>` body (the `marker-line-inside-script-text` case), because deciding provenance needs a real parse the corpus cannot express and because the CSS region's markers legitimately live in the raw-text body of a `<style>`. Whether such a line IS a boundary is decided by every consumer, identically on both sides: `check_layer` cross-checks each counted marker against the document parse (CMH-VAL-20) and the runtime export gate does the same against `DOMParser` (CMH-EXP-22), so a marker a browser does not read is refused by the validator and the runtime alike rather than by one of them. Separately, the DOM-comment locator `_cmhRegionCommentBounds` (`assets/js/84-section-review.js`) is deliberately NOT one of the four: it asks a different question (which comment NODE delimits the region) of a document the HTML parser has already newline-normalized, so it neither reads raw text nor shares these offsets. | `tests/test_region_marker_parity.py` - `test_every_python_copy_answers_the_corpus_identically`, `test_match_ends_cover_exactly_the_marker`, `test_line_breaks_are_the_newlines_the_runtime_sees`, `test_file_readers_preserve_lone_cr_for_the_marker_decision`, `test_validator_file_reader_normalizes_browser_semantic_newlines`, `test_corpus_stays_in_the_basic_multilingual_plane`, `test_a_match_refuses_a_group_it_does_not_have`, `test_fixture_is_non_trivial`; `tests/test_upgrade.py` - `test_source_provenance_offsets_ignore_a_lone_cr_before_an_lf`; `tests/59-region-marker-parity.spec.js` - `the runtime region locator matches the shared parity fixture (CMH-VAL-22)`, `the runtime breaks marker lines on newlines only, like the Python copies (CMH-VAL-22)`; source: `assets/js/67-export-standalone.js` - `_cmhRegionMarkerMatches()`; `tools/validate/validate.py` - `_read()`, `_browser_newlines()`, `_marker_newline_errors()`; `tools/validate/checks/parsing.py` - `_region_marker_matches()`, `_MarkerMatch`, `_line_starts()`; `tools/authoring/upgrade.py` - `_read()`, `_region_marker_matches()`, `_RootSourceFinder`; `dev/tools/build_parts/20-nonshareable-regions.py` - `_region_marker_matches()` |
| CMH-KIND-02 | `new_document.py` and `retrofit.py` require `--kind`, stamp the `commentable-html-kind` meta, and only auto-add a document title from `--label` for kinds that carry one (`report`/`plan` add it when the fragment has none; `slides`/`board` do not). `retrofit.py` detects an existing kind meta by parsing head metadata order-independently (including a non-canonical `content`/`name` attribute order) and REPLACES it in place rather than appending a duplicate, so retrofitting `--kind` onto a host that already declares a kind yields exactly one meta with the requested kind. `new_document.DOC_KINDS` stays in sync with `validate._DOC_KINDS`. | `tests/test_new_document.py` - `KindTests` (`test_doc_kinds_match_validate`, `test_kind_is_required`, `test_kind_stamps_meta`, `test_slides_kind_does_not_add_title`, `test_report_kind_adds_title_and_validates`, `test_make_document_stamps_kind`); `tests/test_retrofit.py` - `test_kind_is_stamped_and_required`, `test_existing_kind_meta_is_replaced_not_duplicated`, `test_reordered_existing_kind_meta_is_replaced` |
| CMH-KIND-03 | `upgrade.py` migrates a document that predates the kind meta by adding a default `generic` kind, so upgrading an older document yields one that declares a kind and validates. It detects an existing kind meta by parsing head metadata order-independently (including a non-canonical `content`/`name` attribute order), so a document that already declares a kind is never given a duplicate meta on upgrade. | `tests/test_upgrade.py` - `test_missing_kind_meta_is_added_on_upgrade`, `test_reordered_kind_meta_is_not_duplicated_on_upgrade` |
| CMH-KIND-04 | `tools/authoring/recommend_kind.py` recommends only `report`, `plan`, or `slides` from filename and content signals: diff fences or `@@` hunks point to reports, comparison tables and callouts point to plans, and slide cadence from repeated `<hr>` dividers or top-level `<h1>` headings points to slides. It prints the recommended `--kind` with evidence, and when a caller supplies a contradictory `--kind` it emits an advisory mismatch warning without changing the caller's chosen kind. | `tests/test_recommend_kind.py` - `RecommendKindTests` (`test_recommends_report_from_diff_signals`, `test_recommends_plan_from_comparison_and_callout_signals`, `test_recommends_slides_from_hr_and_h1_cadence`, `test_filename_hint_contributes_to_the_score`, `test_cli_prints_recommendation_with_evidence`, `test_mismatch_warning_is_advisory`) |
| CMH-KIND-05 | Every document should declare a favicon so a browser tab (especially a full-screen one) shows the commentable-html mark instead of the generic globe. The template shell bakes a `<link rel="icon" href="data:image/svg+xml,...">` into the head, and the validator emits a non-fatal WARNING (enforced under `--strict`, the mandatory finalize path) when a document has no favicon in its head. A favicon is a head-level `<link>` whose `rel`, tokenized the way HTML tokenizes a `rel` list (on ASCII whitespace ONLY, through the shared `link_rel_tokens` every other `rel` reader uses), contains the exact token `icon` (so `icon` and `shortcut icon` count, but `apple-touch-icon` / `mask-icon` do not) with a non-empty `href`; an icon link in the body or with an empty `href` does not satisfy the check. Python's argument-less `str.split()` also splits on the vertical tab U+000B, NBSP and U+001C-U+001F, so `rel="icon<VT>x"` used to satisfy the mandatory check with a link the browser never fetches (the tab still showed the generic globe). The `href` half is read the same way, through a shared `link_href_is_set` both sides call: an `href` counts only if it survives the URL parser's own end trim (every C0 control U+0000-U+001F and space), NOT Python's Unicode set. `str.strip()` reaches past ASCII and takes NBSP, U+2028, U+3000 and U+0085, none of which the URL parser removes, so `<link rel="icon" href="&#xa0;">` - a URL a browser resolves and fetches - was called empty: `--strict` warned `no favicon` about a document that declares one a browser honors, and `retrofit` / `upgrade` injected a second icon beside the author's. The check asks whether an icon is DECLARED, not whether it renders: an `href` the trim empties resolves to the document's OWN url rather than to an icon resource, so an absent `href`, an empty one, and one made only of characters the trim removes all still warn - and, in the other direction, a declared `href` that a browser fetches and gets a 404 for is respected rather than second-guessed (the tools will not add a working icon beside it). The authoring tools inject the CMH favicon when the host lacks one: `retrofit.py` adds it to a host head with no favicon (and never duplicates an existing one), and `upgrade.py` migrates a pre-favicon document by adding the template's favicon. Detection is shared (`tools/authoring/_favicon.py`, an `HTMLParser`-based helper matching the validator, which reads the same `link_rel_tokens` split and the same `link_href_is_set` emptiness test through `tools/_browser_attrs.py` rather than restating either) so the tools inject exactly when the validator would warn: it is token-exact, head-scoped, ignores links inside `<script>` / `<style>` / comments, does not read `data-rel` / `data-href` as `rel` / `href`, and is not vulnerable to the quadratic blowup a raw-regex scan would have. A host declaring only `apple-touch-icon` still gets a real favicon. | `tests/test_validate_kind.py` - `FaviconTests` (`test_favicon_present_is_clean`, `test_missing_favicon_warns`, `test_empty_href_favicon_warns`, `test_shortcut_icon_rel_is_accepted`, `test_apple_touch_icon_does_not_count`, `test_body_only_favicon_warns`, `test_icon_rel_is_tokenized_the_way_html_tokenizes_it`, `test_href_emptiness_is_measured_the_way_a_browser_measures_it`); `tests/test_retrofit.py` - `FaviconHelperTests` (rel-token, HTML-vs-Python `rel` split, HTML-vs-Python `href` emptiness (`test_href_emptiness_is_measured_the_way_html_measures_it`), head-scoping, data-attribute, script/style/comment, and no-quadratic-blowup cases), `test_favicon_is_added_when_host_head_has_none`, `test_existing_host_favicon_is_not_duplicated`, `test_a_non_ascii_whitespace_href_favicon_is_not_duplicated`, `test_apple_touch_icon_only_host_still_gets_a_real_favicon`; `tests/test_upgrade.py` - `test_missing_favicon_is_added_on_upgrade`, `test_existing_favicon_is_not_duplicated_on_upgrade`, `test_a_non_ascii_whitespace_href_favicon_is_not_duplicated_on_upgrade`, `test_apple_touch_icon_only_document_gets_a_real_favicon_on_upgrade`; `tests/test_shared_attr_decoding.py` - `test_the_degraded_path_measures_an_href_the_way_html_measures_it`, `test_the_degraded_href_reading_is_pinned_to_the_shared_one` |
| CMH-TOOL-01 | `tools/validate/validate.py` is a standard-library CLI that validates one or more HTML files, supports `--charts-only` and `--layer-only`, prints usage on `-h`/`--help`, treats a bare `--` as an end-of-options separator (a following dash-led token is a path, not a flag), treats warnings as exit 0 by default, exits non-zero under `--strict` on any BLOCKING warning (an advisory - CMH-VAL-18 - is printed but never fails strict, since the author cannot clear it; the `--help` text and the strict-failure line both say so), and continues through batches. | `tests/test_validate_cli.py` - `ValidateCliTests`, `ValidateMainTests` (`test_help_flag_prints_usage`, `test_double_dash_separator_treats_following_token_as_path`, ...); `tests/test_validate_self_contained.py` - `NewCheckTests` `--strict`; `tests/test_validate_charts.py` - `ChartValidatorTests.test_cli_ok_exit_zero`, `ChartValidatorTests.test_cli_warnings_only_exit_zero`, `ChartValidatorTests.test_cli_error_exit_one`, `ChartValidatorTests.test_cli_batch_mixed_exits_one`, `ChartValidatorTests.test_cli_usage_exit_two` |
| CMH-HL-02 | `tools/blocks/highlight_document.py` bakes syntax highlighting into every raw, language-labelled `<pre><code class="language-XXX">` block of a file in one pass (aliases resolved, e.g. `cs` -> `csharp`), rewriting only blocks whose language the highlighter supports and whose inner is still raw (no `cmh-code-*` spans, no markup); an already-highlighted block, an inline `<code>`, a non-highlightable label, and an empty block are left untouched, and re-running is a no-op. It exposes a `--check` mode (exit 1 if any block needs highlighting) and a `-` stdin/stdout mode. `tools/authoring/finalize.py` runs this step by DEFAULT (opt out with `--no-highlight`) before validation, so the standard finalization bakes highlighting. | `tests/test_highlight_document.py` - full unit suite (`HighlightDocumentTests`); `tests/test_finalize.py` - `test_highlights_code_blocks_by_default`, `test_no_highlight_flag_skips_highlighting`; source: `tools/blocks/highlight_document.py`, `tools/authoring/finalize.py` |
| CMH-HL-04 | The document-producing tools bake syntax highlighting by DEFAULT (opt out with `--no-highlight`) and surface validator warnings instead of discarding them, so a freshly created document is never raw and a skipped-highlight/skip warning is visible at creation. This is the root-cause prevention for a raw `language-XXX` block shipping unbaked because baking used to live only in the separate, manual finalize step. `new_document.py` and `deck_scaffold.py` bake then print warnings (they do not block on warnings); `retrofit.py` bakes then fails closed on any FATAL validator warning (an advisory, CMH-VAL-18, is reported but does not block), so it never writes a raw document - in Shareable and NonShareable mode alike, which needs the author-time highlighting check to scan only the AUTHORED markup (CMH-VAL-11's scan boundary), or the guardrail is blind exactly where the inlined layer prose mentions `<pre>`/`<code>`. | `tests/test_new_document.py` - `test_cli_bakes_syntax_highlighting_by_default`, `test_cli_no_highlight_flag_leaves_code_raw`, `test_cli_surfaces_validator_warnings`; `tests/test_retrofit.py` - `test_bakes_syntax_highlighting_by_default`, `test_no_highlight_with_raw_code_is_blocked_by_validation` (both layer modes); `tests/test_deck_example.py` - `test_scaffold_bakes_highlighting_by_default`, `test_scaffold_no_highlight_leaves_raw_and_warns`; source: `tools/authoring/new_document.py`, `tools/authoring/retrofit.py`, `tools/deck/deck_scaffold.py` |
| CMH-HL-07 | `markdown` (aliases `md`, `mdown`, `mkd`) is a supported highlighting language. Markdown carries no keywords, so `highlight_code.py` dispatches it to a dedicated line-oriented tokenizer that reuses the six shipped `cmh-code-*` classes: ATX headings, setext underlines (`=`, and a `--`-or-longer run under paragraph text) and bold and a fence info string are `kw`; emphasis, strikethrough and HTML comments are `com`; inline code spans (1 to 3 backticks), a fenced-code body whose info string names no known language, autolinks and link/reference destinations are `str`; link and image text, reference labels and footnote references are `fn`; ordered-list marker digits are `num`; and structural punctuation (fence delimiters, blockquote `>`, list bullets, task checkboxes, thematic breaks, table pipes and delimiter rows, link brackets, inline HTML tags) is `op`. A fenced body is tokenized in the language its INFO STRING names - the first word, aliases resolved, so ` ```py ` and ` ```python title="x.py" ` both select Python - and falls back to one opaque `str` run when the label is unknown (`kusto`, `text`) or absent. The body is buffered and tokenized as ONE unit when the fence closes, so a multi-line construct inside it (a C-style block comment, a Python triple-quoted string) reads exactly as it would in a standalone block of that language; the nested body therefore inherits that language's accepted author-time-versus-runtime tokenizer differences (operator-run grouping, the runtime's smaller keyword set) and nesting adds no new divergence. Markdown is the one language that can nest into itself, so a ` ```markdown ` body recurses through a DEPTH BOUND (`_MD_MAX_NESTING`) and past it stays opaque - a hostile document of thousands of ` ```markdown ` openers cannot exhaust the stack. An HTML comment carries across lines like a fence; an intraword underscore (`some_long_name`) never starts emphasis, a backslash-escaped marker stays literal, and an unterminated or space-padded marker (`**bold **`, CommonMark right-flanking) is left plain. Every scan that can fail is length-capped, so adversarial input (a line of 30k `[` or `<`) cannot blow up quadratically in the browser. Deliberate simplifications, both of which need full block parsing to do properly: a 4-space indented code block and YAML front matter are tokenized as prose. Like every other language the rendered text is the source character for character and every character is escaped exactly once. | `tests/test_highlight_markdown.py` - full unit suite (`MarkdownLanguageRegistrationTests`, `MarkdownBlockTokenTests`, `MarkdownInlineTokenTests`, `MarkdownFalsePositiveTests`, `MarkdownFidelityTests`, `MarkdownPerformanceTests`); `tests/test_highlight_golden.py` - `test_highlight_matches_preannotated_golden`, `test_golden_text_roundtrips_to_the_sample` (the `markdown` golden pair); source: `tools/blocks/highlight_code.py` - `_highlight_markdown()`, `_md_fence_language()` |
| CMH-STAMP-01 | The document-producing tools stamp a `<meta name="commentable-html-created">` provenance timestamp so the runtime can tell a produced document apart from a strict-validated one. `new_document.py` and `deck_scaffold.py` stamp it (idempotently, honoring `--generated` for deterministic metadata); creation never claims validation (no validated stamp is written at creation). Meta insertion and removal preserve the document's dominant newline, including CRLF. | `tests/test_doc_stamp.py` - `DocStampUnitTests` (set/get/insert/idempotent/escape/newline preservation), `test_new_document_stamps_created`, `test_new_document_does_not_claim_validation`; source: `tools/authoring/doc_stamp.py`, `tools/authoring/new_document.py`, `tools/deck/deck_scaffold.py` |
| CMH-STAMP-02 | `validate.py` and `finalize.py` write a `<meta name="commentable-html-validated">` timestamp AND a `<meta name="commentable-html-validated-hash">` content signature into the file ONLY on a STRICT-CLEAN FULL pass (no errors AND no BLOCKING warnings, and both the layer and chart halves ran - a `--charts-only`/`--layer-only` partial run never stamps), so the validated stamp means the document actually passed the whole strict validator. Stamping works when `validate.py` runs as a standalone CLI subprocess (it puts the sibling `authoring/` tools on `sys.path` via the shared bootstrap so `_stamp_validated_file`'s `import doc_stamp` resolves), not only in-process; `validate.py` is not read-only. A CRLF document remains uniformly CRLF when those missing meta tags are inserted. A stamp-write failure is a visible NOTE, never silent, and never blocks validation. `--no-stamp` keeps a pure/CI run read-only (no write); a doc with a BLOCKING warning is not stamped, while a doc whose only remaining warnings are ADVISORIES (CMH-VAL-18) IS stamped - an advisory names something the author cannot clear, so withholding the stamp would leave the runtime `not validated` banner up forever. The stamp WRITE is ATOMIC: both `validate.py` and the single write `finalize.py` makes stage the stamped bytes in the target's own directory and swap them in with `os.replace` (shared `_atomic_io.atomic_write`, CMH-PORT-04), so an interrupted or failing write leaves the ORIGINAL bytes untouched instead of truncating a document that just passed validation - the old `open(path, "w")` truncated first, and because stamping is best-effort the loss was reported as only a NOTE. A partial install of `validate.py` that cannot import `_atomic_io` refuses to stamp (a visible missing-tool warning) rather than falling back to a truncating write; `finalize.py` imports it up front like its other siblings, so a partial install fails there loudly instead. Replacing rather than rewriting inherits CMH-PORT-04's declared residuals: the stamped document is a NEW inode carrying the original's mode, so ownership/ACLs and hard-link identity are not carried over, and on POSIX it is the DIRECTORY's write permission that permits the swap. | `tests/test_doc_stamp.py` - `test_validate_stamps_validated_on_clean_file`, `test_validate_stamps_a_crlf_file_without_mixing_newlines`, `test_validate_no_stamp_flag_leaves_file_unstamped`, `test_validate_does_not_stamp_a_doc_with_warnings`, `test_validate_partial_modes_never_stamp`, `test_validate_cli_subprocess_stamps_validated_and_content_hash`, `test_validate_cli_subprocess_no_stamp_flag_is_read_only`, `test_validate_reports_a_visible_note_when_stamping_fails_but_still_passes`, `AtomicStampWriteTests` (`test_an_interrupted_stamp_write_leaves_the_original_document_intact`, `test_the_stamp_never_opens_the_validated_document_for_truncation`, `test_a_missing_atomic_io_refuses_to_stamp_rather_than_truncating`); `tests/test_finalize.py` - `test_stamps_validated_on_a_strict_clean_finalize`, `test_no_stamp_flag_skips_the_validated_stamp`, `test_an_interrupted_finalize_write_leaves_the_original_document_intact`; source: `tools/validate/validate.py` - `_stamp_validated_file`, `tools/authoring/finalize.py` |
| CMH-STAMP-03 | On load, the runtime shows a small, dismissible amber fallback banner when a document carries a `commentable-html-created` stamp but no CURRENT `commentable-html-validated` stamp - the stamp is absent, older than the created stamp, OR content-bound (has a `commentable-html-validated-hash`) whose hash no longer matches the live document's authored TEXT (a post-validation text edit). A strict-validated document whose authored text still matches, and any document with no created stamp (older/foreign), show nothing. The banner is `cm-skip` chrome added to `CMH_INJECTED_CHROME`, so it never bakes into a Save/Export snapshot and is re-derived on load. This is a last resort; the skill MUST always finalize and strict-validate. | `tests/64-validation-banner.spec.js` - `an unvalidated document shows the fallback banner`, `a validated document shows no banner`, `a validation older than creation still shows the banner`, `a document with no created stamp shows no banner`, `the banner is dismissible`, `the banner does not leak into a Plain export`; source: `assets/js/38-validation-banner.js`, `assets/css/87-validation-banner.css` |
| CMH-STAMP-04 | The document-producing tools stamp the creating AI agent's session id as `<meta name="commentable-html-session-id">` (and its agent slug as `commentable-html-agent`) so the footer can copy it (CMH-FOOT-04). `new_document.py` and `deck_scaffold.py` stamp it BY DEFAULT from `--session-id` or, when that is absent, an auto-detected environment variable (`COPILOT_AGENT_SESSION_ID` -> copilot, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`/`CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` -> claude); `--agent` overrides the slug and `--no-session-id` opts out. When several agents' session ids are visible at once (a nested launch), the agent that is the immediate runtime wins via its marker env var (`CLAUDECODE` vs `COPILOT_CLI`). The session stamp is idempotent (a re-scaffold preserves the original producer) and a blank id is a no-op. | `tests/test_doc_stamp.py` - `DocStampUnitTests` (`test_stamp_session_sets_id_and_agent`, `test_stamp_session_is_idempotent`, `test_stamp_session_blank_is_noop`, `test_detect_session_reads_copilot_env`, `test_detect_session_reads_claude_env`, `test_detect_session_reads_claude_legacy_env`, `test_detect_session_prefers_copilot_when_both_present_and_no_runtime_marker`, `test_detect_session_prefers_the_running_agent_when_both_present`, `test_detect_session_absent_is_none`), `NewDocumentSessionStampTests`; `tests/test_deck_scaffold.py` - `test_scaffold_stamps_session_from_flag`, `test_scaffold_stamps_session_from_environment_by_default`, `test_scaffold_no_session_id_flag_suppresses`; source: `tools/authoring/doc_stamp.py`, `tools/authoring/new_document.py`, `tools/deck/deck_scaffold.py`; local live checks (not CI; drive the real agent CLIs, shared core `tests/_session_e2e.py`): `tests/copilot_e2e_check.py`, `tests/claude_e2e_check.py` |
| CMH-STAMP-05 | The validated stamp is CONTENT-BOUND to the document's authored TEXT: `validate.py`/`finalize.py` write `commentable-html-validated-hash`, a whole content-root text hash computed with the shared section-hash contract (`section_hash.document_content_hash` - the same FNV-1a and cm-skip/script/style/template/noscript/diff/KQL/mermaid/canvas/note exclusion the section-review hashes use, over the first `id=commentRoot` subtree only, matching the runtime `getElementById`), and the runtime (`cmhDocContentHash` in `84-section-review.js`, exposed as `window.__cmhReview.docHash()`) reproduces it byte for byte. It is a stable-text fingerprint (a strong nudge, not a cryptographic seal): a post-validation edit to the visible authored TEXT re-shows the banner and re-validation clears it, but attribute-only edits and edits inside the excluded rendered blocks are not tracked. A document with no content root gets a timestamp-only stamp (no hash) and the runtime falls back to the timestamp signal so it never false-positives; a reader's persisted table sort is canonicalized (the scan READS the stamped source row order without moving any row) so it does not falsely invalidate, while a genuine content rearrangement (widget/board drag baked into an export) legitimately does. `finalize.py` prints a guardrail reminder on a strict-fail that the fix must end with a clean strict pass to re-stamp. | `tests/test_section_hash_golden.py` - `DocumentContentHashTests`; `tests/test_doc_stamp.py` - `ValidatedHashStampTests`, `test_validate_stamps_content_hash_matching_the_document`, `test_validate_cli_subprocess_stamps_validated_and_content_hash`, `test_validate_partial_modes_never_stamp`; `tests/test_finalize.py` - `test_warnings_fail_with_strict`; `tests/64-validation-banner.spec.js` - `the runtime exposes a stable whole-document content hash`, `a validated document whose content hash matches shows no banner`, `a validated document whose content hash no longer matches shows the banner`, `validate.py content-binds the stamp end to end (Python/JS hash parity, edit re-shows, re-validate clears)`, `the Python and runtime document hashes agree on rich content (kitchen-sink parity)`, `in-root noscript is excluded from the hash on both sides (JS/Python parity)`, `a persisted table sort does not falsely invalidate the stamp (canonical hash)`, `a persisted sort of a whitespace-formatted table does not falsely invalidate the stamp`, `authored data-cmh-row attributes cannot redefine the canonical order (JS/Python parity)`; source: `tools/authoring/section_hash.py` - `document_content_hash`, `tools/authoring/doc_stamp.py` - `stamp_validated_html`, `assets/js/84-section-review.js` - `cmhDocContentHash`, `assets/js/38-validation-banner.js` |

| CMH-TOOL-07 | `tools/authoring/new_document.py` produces a **Shareable** document (one self-contained file) by default - `--shareable` merely names that default and `--nonshareable` is accepted and ignored; a legacy **NonShareable** document is built deliberately with `--template <dist>/NONSHAREABLE.html`, whose companion refs are repointed to the skill's `dist/` (`--copy-assets` copies them next to the file, `--assets-href` sets a custom prefix, `--assets-relative` uses a relative path). It replaces only the CONTENT fragment and the `#commentRoot` data attributes, refuses demo keys (except when re-stamping an existing document via `allow_reserved_key`), updates the `<title>`, reads a fragment from a file or UTF-8 stdin, and self-validates before writing. NonShareable output to stdout (no `--out`) uses absolute `file://` companion refs by default, while `--assets-href` and `--copy-assets` keep their explicit modes. When the generated document cannot be CHECKED at all - the validator is unimportable (including a partial install where `validate.py` is present but one of its own `checks.*` imports is not), a truncated `validate.py` that raises on import, a `validate` module resolved from OUTSIDE this skill's tools dir (compared canonically, so a junction or a differently-cased path does not false-alarm, while a `__file__` that is not a usable path string - a non-path value, a `PathLike` whose `__fspath__` misbehaves, a path that cannot be canonicalized, a `__file__` whose ACCESS raises, or one that cannot be formatted into the message - is refused with that same named reason rather than raising), a validator that crashes, or one that answers in an unexpected shape (notably `(None, None)`, which would otherwise read as "no errors, no warnings") - the tool FAILS CLOSED: nothing is written to `--out` or stdout and it exits non-zero naming the actual cause, because writing a document whose one promise (it validates) could not be checked is the wrong direction exactly where something is already wrong. A caller who knowingly accepts unchecked output passes `--allow-unvalidated-output`, which writes it with a warning; that flag never suppresses a real validation failure. The content root's start tag is READ through the shared browser reading (`_browser_attrs.raw_attrs_pairs`, CMH-VAL-21) and REWRITTEN through the shared writer (`_browser_attrs.serialize_start_tag`), rather than through a local `([^\s=/<>]+)` pattern whose name class cannot even REPRESENT a name containing `=`: that reading turned the inert `=onclick` a browser sees (HTML5's unexpected-equals-sign-before-attribute-name state) into a live `onclick` handler on the way out, and the matching bare-name WRITE let two adjacent valueless attributes fuse into one carrying a value the template never had (#1195). A valueless attribute is therefore written back as `name=""` - the same attribute to a browser, so the generated root carries `data-cmh-content-root=""` where it used to carry the bare name and every gate still reads it by presence - and a value is decoded before it is re-escaped, so an authored `A &amp; B` round-trips instead of being written back as the literal `A &amp;amp; B`. It is correctness, not a vulnerability: the document's own author is trusted (CMH-SEC-01) and the input here is the shipped template. | `tests/test_new_document.py` - clone, content swap, demo-key rejection, allow-reserved-key, active-root-attrs, stdin, title, NonShareable template/`--copy-assets`/`--assets-href`, `test_nonshareable_stdout_uses_absolute_file_urls`, `test_nonshareable_stdout_ok_with_assets_href`, `test_nonshareable_stdout_ok_when_shareable`, `--shareable`, mode mutual-exclusion, validation tests, `test_self_validate_reports_the_cause_when_the_validator_is_missing`, and `NewDocumentUnvalidatedOutputTests` (an unimportable validator naming its cause, no file written on the fail-closed path, a partial install missing the validator's own dependency, a corrupt validator source, a cached foreign validate module, a foreign validate on the import path refused before its body runs, a cached module without a file path, a cached module whose file path is not a string, `test_a_misbehaving_pathlike_file_is_refused_rather_than_raising`, `test_an_unformattable_module_file_still_comes_back_as_a_reason`, `test_contained_refuses_a_non_path_value_without_raising`, `test_a_path_that_cannot_be_canonicalized_is_refused_rather_than_raising`, `test_a_module_whose_file_attribute_raises_is_refused_rather_than_raising`, the real validator still accepted (including a parent-walking and a differently-cased spelling), a crashing validator, an unexpected return shape, the opt-out writing with a warning, the opt-out NOT suppressing a real validation failure, and a working validator still validating under the flag), `test_a_valueless_root_attribute_cannot_fuse_with_the_next_one`, `test_a_root_attribute_name_beginning_with_equals_is_not_renamed`, `test_a_root_attribute_value_is_escaped_exactly_once` |
| CMH-TOOL-08 | `tools/authoring/upgrade.py` upgrades a standalone document by replacing the CSS, COMMENT UI, and JS regions (preserving handled ids, embedded comments, content, and the root) and re-emitting the shell-baked mermaid loader bootstrap (see CMH-MMD-09), validates the result in a temp file, and leaves the target unchanged if validation fails or the validator crashes. It also restamps the `<head>` `<meta name="commentable-html-version">` to the template's runtime version (inserting it when a pre-version legacy document lacks it) so an upgraded file no longer self-reports the old version; the restamp is scoped to `<head>` so the marker-like JS export regex literal (`content="[^"]+"`) is never matched or rewritten. Post-upgrade validator warnings are surfaced (not discarded) and the upgrade still commits by default so a version-only upgrade is never blocked by a pre-existing content warning; the opt-in `--strict` flag treats those warnings as a failure, leaving the target unchanged and exiting non-zero. A region boundary is matched only on a COMPLETE marker line (the keyword phrase alone on its line, optionally wrapped by its comment delimiter and/or a `====` banner, with nothing else on the line), so an inline mention or a line-leading marker phrase carrying trailing authored text cannot corrupt a swap. The shipped upgrader, maintainer build tool, and runtime export slicers reject duplicate BEGIN or END markers for every layer region instead of silently slicing through authored content. It also preserves the input file's DOMINANT newline (detected from the raw bytes) through the transform, so a Windows-authored (CRLF) document is not silently normalized to LF on upgrade; newline-style differences alone do not make an up-to-date CRLF file stale, and output never doubles a retained carriage return. An unreadable or invalid-UTF-8 input exits 2 with a diagnostic instead of a traceback. The swap also PRESERVES THE DESTINATION'S FILE MODE: the validated staging file comes from `mkstemp` (POSIX `0600`) and `os.replace` carries the staged inode's mode to the target, so without the mode step an upgraded `0644` report would silently become owner-only. The shared `_atomic_io.preserve_mode` copies the destination's `0777` permission bits onto the staged file just before the replace (special setuid/setgid/sticky bits are never copied onto a freshly staged inode, and a destination that exists but cannot be statted is left alone so a guess can never WIDEN a private file); a `--out` to a path that does not exist yet has no mode to preserve, so it takes the SOURCE document's mode rather than a process default that could widen a private report. The destination is resolved with `realpath` first (as `atomic_write` does), so upgrading through a symlink rewrites the real document instead of replacing the link with a regular file and stranding it, and cleanup goes through `_atomic_io.quiet_remove` so a staged file that inherited a read-only mode cannot leak. | `tests/test_upgrade.py` - region swap, preservation, stale `--check`, validation-failure-leaves-target-unchanged, validator-crash-surfaced, marker-line tests, duplicate BEGIN/END tests, `test_version_meta_is_bumped_to_template_version`, `test_version_meta_is_inserted_when_missing`, `test_version_meta_noop_when_already_current`, `test_version_meta_bump_ignores_js_regex_literal`, `test_cli_surfaces_warnings_without_strict`, `test_cli_strict_aborts_on_warning_and_leaves_target_unchanged`, `test_cli_strict_commits_when_clean`, `test_cli_preserves_crlf_line_endings`, `test_cli_keeps_lf_when_lf_is_dominant_despite_a_stray_crlf`, `test_cli_check_reports_a_crlf_document_up_to_date`, `test_cli_invalid_utf8_returns_2_without_a_traceback`, `test_cli_preserves_the_target_file_mode`, `test_cli_preserves_an_executable_target_file_mode`, `test_cli_out_to_an_existing_file_preserves_that_file_mode`, `test_cli_out_to_a_new_file_takes_the_source_file_mode`, `test_cli_upgrades_through_a_symlink_instead_of_replacing_it`, `test_cli_leaves_no_temp_file_when_the_replace_itself_fails`; `tests/test_build.py` - `BuildTests.test_region_inner_rejects_duplicate_begin_marker`, `BuildTests.test_region_inner_rejects_duplicate_end_marker`, `BuildTests.test_region_inner_ignores_marker_text_inside_pre_content`; `tests/06-save-export.spec.js` - `Export plain strips the layer but keeps the content`; `tests/52-region-guard.spec.js` - `Export Plain aborts on a duplicated EMBEDDED COMMENTS BEGIN marker (F28)`, `Export Plain aborts on a duplicated EMBEDDED COMMENTS END marker (F37)`, `Export Offline aborts on a duplicated EMBEDDED COMMENTS BEGIN marker in a nonshareable doc (F28)`, `Export Offline aborts on a duplicated EMBEDDED COMMENTS END marker in a nonshareable doc (F37)` |

| CMH-TOOL-16 | The author-time Python highlighter (`tools/blocks/highlight_code.py`) and the runtime JS diff highlighter (`cmhHighlightCode`) are parallel implementations that must not drift: both classify the shared parity fixture (`tests/fixtures/highlight_parity.json`) identically, in particular an unterminated single quote (a Rust lifetime, a YAML/Ruby apostrophe) is never swallowed as a string to end of line (the PR #33 regression class). | CMH-TOOL-11 | `tools/authoring/generate_toc.py` creates or replaces `nav.cm-toc` under `#commentRoot`, injects missing `h2`/`h3` ids, de-dupes against existing ids, strips a redundant leading author section number from each entry and writes the entry's own number into it as a `cm-skip` `span.cm-toc-num` - the number the HEADING displays when the document numbers its own sections, else a hierarchical one computed from the heading depth - so the Contents list and the side menu show one number for the same heading rather than two that disagree (CMH-TOC-10), and preserves the dominant newline style. The nav is placed BELOW the document title and its reading-time strip: after the top-level title container inside `#commentRoot` (the `<h1>` itself or its wrapper, e.g. `header.cmh-lede`) and after a direct-child `div[data-cmh-doc-stats]` overview strip (CMH-STATS-01) that IMMEDIATELY follows that title, which is the only shape `doc_stats.py` produces - so a reader meets the title and the reading time before the contents list. The TITLE is what earns the move: a strip placed anywhere else never drags the nav down to it. A candidate anchor is discarded - and the nav keeps the top-of-`#commentRoot` placement - when it lands inside the `.cm-toc` being replaced (so the insert and the removal can never overlap; an anchor exactly AT the removal's start is adjacent, not overlapping, and is kept), when its element was not closed by its OWN end tag (an ancestor's closer, an implicit close, or end of input leaves the extent INSIDE the open element), or when it would land after the first section the nav lists (a slide deck or single-wrapper document whose title shares a container with its sections, or a document whose first section precedes its title). A nav that lists NO sections is held to the same rule rather than exempted from it, since with nothing to measure against the title container's extent is exactly as misleading. A document with no `<h1>` keeps the top-of-root placement too, an existing nav above the title is MOVED down rather than duplicated, and the rewrite is idempotent for every shape this row describes. A heading inside the `nav.cm-toc` the rewrite REPLACES is navigation chrome: it is never listed, never receives a generated id, and its own ids do not reserve a slug, so the rewrite stays lossless and idempotent instead of queueing an id insertion inside the span it deletes; any two rewrite spans that did overlap are refused rather than applied. The rule is scoped to the replaced span, so a `.cm-toc` the rewrite leaves in place (one closed by an ancestor's end tag or by end of input, or one wrapping or being `#commentRoot`) still has its headings listed rather than silently yielding an empty table of contents. Each id is recorded with its element offset as a single pair, so the surviving-id set cannot silently desynchronize and generate an id that duplicates a surviving one. | `tests/test_generate_toc.py` - | CMH-TOOL-17 | `tools/authoring/wrap_sections.py` is the deterministic auto-fix for the flat-section warning (CMH-VAL-14): it wraps each bare top-level `<h2>` block (the `<h2>` plus following siblings up to the next top-level `<h2>`) in `<section aria-labelledby="the-h2-id">` so `report`/`plan` documents render as boxed section cards; content before the first top-level `<h2>` (title, `cmh-lede`) stays above the cards. | CMH-TOOL-19 | `tools/authoring/new_document.py`, `tools/authoring/retrofit.py`, and `tools/deck/deck_scaffold.py` accept `--brand path/to/brand.json` as an opt-in, stateless theme profile. | CMH-TOOL-22 | Every tool that rewrites a file the user could lose goes through the shared crash-safe writer at the `tools/` ROOT (`_atomic_io`, CMH-PORT-04), which stages the new bytes in the target's own directory and swaps them in with `os.replace`. In-place document rewrites: `authoring/mark_handled.py`, `mark_reviewed.py`, `generate_toc.py`, `doc_stats.py`, `normalize_typography.py`, `wrap_sections.py`, `fix_skip.py`, `inline_images.py` and `vendored_libs.py`, `notes/notes_apply.py`, `checklist/checklist_apply.py`, `blocks/highlight_document.py`, and `deck/deck_fix_fonts.py`. Caller-named `--out` targets, which can name a file that already holds the user's bytes: `authoring/content_extract.py`, `notes/notes_scaffold.py`, `checklist/checklist_scaffold.py`, `deck/pptx_to_fragment.py`, `deck/deck_scaffold.py`, and `authoring/new_document.py --force` (without `--force` it is an EXCLUSIVE create that refuses an existing file, so there is nothing to lose). The NonShareable companions `new_document --copy-assets` refreshes beside a document are copied the same way (`_atomic_io.stage_copy` + `commit_staged`), because a half-copied runtime or stylesheet breaks a document just as effectively; the whole SET is staged before any of it is swapped in, so a failure DURING staging cannot leave a document pairing a new runtime with an old stylesheet (the renames themselves are still separate - see the residuals below). A copy does NOT inherit its source's mode by default: a companion's source is the skill's own installed `dist/`, whose permissions say how the plugin was extracted rather than what the user wants beside their report, and a read-only one would land a companion that could never be refreshed again. SKILL.md points an agent straight at a real document, so the truncating `open(path, "w")` these used emptied the user's only copy BEFORE the replacement bytes existed: a full disk, an encoding error, a killed run or a Ctrl+C left half a document, or none. A failed or interrupted write now leaves the original byte for byte, leaks no staged file, and is reported rather than silently half-applied; a destination whose DIRECTORY cannot be written is reported in terms of the document and the directory rather than as a failure on a `.cmh-write-` path the user has never seen. A CLI writing to `--out` names its SOURCE document (or, for a scaffold or a converter, its input file when it is not stdin) as the mode fallback, so a transformed copy written to a path that does not exist yet keeps the source's visibility - capped by the mode a plain create would produce, so it is never wider than either the source or the umask allows. The writer lives at the `tools/` ROOT rather than in `authoring/`, because tools in five buckets plus the validator's stamp now depend on it and a shared guarantee must not be reachable only from inside one bucket; the three tools that hand-roll their own staging (`authoring/upgrade.py`, `authoring/retrofit.py`, `deck/deck_theme.py`) share the swap itself through `_atomic_io.commit_staged` and flush their staged bytes with `_atomic_io.fsync_file`, so the durability and the retry are not properties only `atomic_write` callers get - a rename is only as durable as the bytes it points at. A NEW tool cannot quietly reintroduce the truncating write: a static guard parses every shipped tool and FAILS CLOSED on any call that can empty a named file - `open`/`io.open`/`Path.open` with a truncating mode OR with a mode that is not a literal (the `"w" if force else "x"` spelling that hid one), `Path.write_text`/`write_bytes`, `shutil.copyfile`/`copy`/`copy2`/`move` (including when imported under a bare or renamed name), and `os.open` whose flags are not provably read-only - unless the call is marked (`# atomic-write: <reason>`) as a write to a file the tool staged itself and so has no bytes to lose. The guard's own detection surface is pinned by a test, both the spellings it must catch and the ones it must NOT (a `dict.copy()` is not a file write, and a guard that cries wolf gets silenced). Each behavioral case also proves the sabotaged run REACHED the shared writer for its target, so a tool that aborts earlier cannot pass the case vacuously. Replacing rather than rewriting inherits CMH-PORT-04's residuals (the document is a NEW inode carrying the original's mode, so ownership/ACLs and hard-link identity are not carried over; because it is the DIRECTORY's write permission that permits the swap, a document the user marked read-only can now be replaced where a truncating write would have failed, and a document in a directory the user cannot write can no longer be rewritten at all; and a hard kill in the window between the mode step and the swap can strand a `.cmh-write-` copy of the document carrying the destination's mode). | `tests/test_atomic_document_writes.py` - `AtomicDocumentWriteTests` (`test_an_interrupted_write_leaves_the_document_untouched`), `StagedWorkFileTests` (`test_a_keyboard_interrupt_mid_transaction_leaves_no_work_file`), `NoTruncatingWriteTests` (`test_no_shipped_tool_truncates_a_file_it_did_not_stage`, `test_the_guard_detects_every_truncating_spelling_it_claims_to`); `tests/test_atomic_io.py` - `ReplaceRetryTests`, `AtomicCopyTests`, `test_atomic_write_passes_the_fallback_through_to_a_new_destination`; `tests/test_tools_layout.py` - `test_tools_root_holds_only_the_bootstrap_and_buckets`; source: `tools/_atomic_io.py` |
| CMH-TOOL-23 | A tool never lands a write on a file it READ as a DIFFERENT artifact: every destination is compared against every file input the run will read, and an alias is REFUSED with a non-zero exit before the write, rather than replacing that input with the derived artifact and exiting 0. The hazard is per INPUT, not per tool, and an input is not always named on the command line. Derived-output tools: `authoring/content_extract.py` (the document it extracts the CONTENT region from), `checklist/checklist_scaffold.py` (`--in`), `deck/pptx_to_fragment.py` (`--input` / `--pptx`), `deck/deck_scaffold.py` (`--content`, `--brand`, `--theme`, plus the shipped `dist/SHAREABLE.html` and `viewport-base.css` it reads off its own install), and `authoring/new_document.py` (`--content`, the RESOLVED `--template` so the skill's own `dist/SHAREABLE.html` cannot be overwritten, and `--brand`). Tools that legitimately rewrite their document IN PLACE are guarded on their OTHER inputs, since the in-place one is the same artifact: `authoring/upgrade.py` (`--template`, checked immediately before the write so the read-only `--check` and already-up-to-date runs are never refused for a write they do not make), `authoring/retrofit.py` (`--brand` and BOTH templates it reads - Shareable mode consults the NonShareable one for its theme variables), `deck/deck_theme.py` (`--theme`). A `--theme` is compared as the file `load` would READ - a bare preset NAME resolves into the shipped `themes/` directory - so the comparison neither misses the preset the run reads nor false-alarms on an unrelated output that happens to share the name. Destinations the caller never spells are checked too: `new_document --copy-assets` and `retrofit --copy-assets` write three companions on FIXED names beside the document, and each is checked before any of them is staged; and `authoring/content_replace.py` rewrites a POSITIONAL document, so its `--content` and `--handled-from-bundle` are checked against it (there the aliasing run was already rejected by the fragment-marker check, so this is a precise error rather than a new protection). The comparison is CANONICAL, not textual (`_atomic_io.same_file`: `os.path.samefile` when both paths exist, falling back to a `realpath` + `normcase` comparison, and answering False rather than raising on a path the platform cannot represent), so a symlink to the input, a `sub/../doc.html` spelling, or a differently-cased path on a case-insensitive filesystem is caught too - the symlink being the dangerous one, since the shared writer follows a symlink to its target (CMH-TOOL-22) and would land on the real document while the destination path itself looked distinct. The refusal names the destination, the input, and the file they resolve to. An input that is not an existing FILE is skipped, because a tool cannot lose what it never read, which is what makes an absent optional input and a directory-valued argument answer "no alias" with no per-caller special case; an argument that genuinely means stdin is stripped by the CALLER through `_atomic_io.not_stdin`, so a file genuinely named `-` passed where a path is expected is still a file and is still protected. It never over-refuses: an in-place transform may still write its `--out` over its own input (`normalize_typography --out <input>`), and `new_document` WITHOUT `--force` compares the RESOLVED target, so a run that `resolve_output_path` has already redirected to a suffixed sibling still succeeds. A NEW tool cannot skip the check: a static guard fails any shipped tool (walked recursively and keyed by its path under `tools/`, never by basename) that takes an `--out` and neither refuses an aliasing destination nor is listed as needing no check with the reason. The guard is deliberately strict about what counts: the refusal must be the whole test of an `if` INSIDE the tool's `main`, whose branch returns non-zero, raises, or exits - a call whose bool is ignored, an inverted or conditional test that lets an aliasing run fall through, and a call parked in an unrelated helper all read as UNGUARDED - and its detection surface is pinned by a test of what it must catch and what it must not. Residuals: `authoring/inline_images.py` is exempt because the images it reads are discovered from the document rather than named on the command line, and their bytes survive base64-encoded inside the document it writes; and `same_file`'s fallback does not detect a HARD LINK, which costs nothing here because the writer swaps in a NEW inode, so the other name keeps the original bytes either way. | `tests/test_out_aliases_input.py` - `test_a_destination_that_resolves_to_an_input_is_refused`, `test_a_destination_that_aliases_a_file_read_off_the_install_is_refused`, `test_a_read_only_mode_is_never_refused`, `test_a_distinct_destination_is_never_refused`, `test_a_distinct_out_still_produces_the_artifact`, `test_an_in_place_tool_may_still_write_its_out_over_its_own_input`, `test_new_document_without_force_still_writes_a_suffixed_sibling`, `test_copy_assets_companions_cannot_land_on_an_input`, `test_retrofit_copy_assets_companions_cannot_land_on_an_input`, `test_same_file_is_canonical_not_textual`, `test_same_file_follows_a_symlink_to_its_target`, `test_same_file_folds_case_where_the_filesystem_does`, `test_two_different_files_are_not_the_same_file`, `test_a_path_the_platform_cannot_represent_answers_no_rather_than_raising`, `test_an_input_that_is_not_a_file_is_never_an_alias`, `test_a_file_genuinely_named_dash_is_still_protected`, `test_the_message_names_both_paths_and_the_resolved_file`, `test_there_are_tools_with_an_out_option_to_check`, `test_every_tool_with_an_out_option_checks_it_or_is_listed_as_in_place`, `test_the_in_place_list_names_only_tools_that_exist_and_take_an_out`, `test_the_guard_detects_every_spelling_it_claims_to`; source: `tools/_atomic_io.py` |
| CMH-TOOL-13 | `tools/blocks/chart_block.py` emits a validator-clean `figure.chart` + CDN-failure-guarded Chart.js loader + chart init script from a Chart.js spec JSON; `<` is escaped as `\u003c` in the spec JSON; `aria-label` is derived from title and caption; invalid canvas ids and non-object specs are rejected at the CLI boundary. It adds its own tools dir to `sys.path` on import so the sibling `validate` module is importable and self-validation is never silently skipped under a non-standard invocation. When the fragments cannot be CHECKED at all - the validator is unimportable (including a partial install where `validate.py` is present but one of its own `checks.*` imports is not), a `validate` module resolved from OUTSIDE this skill's tools dir (compared canonically, so a junction or a differently-cased path does not false-alarm, and a cached module whose `__file__` is not a str/PathLike is refused with that same named reason instead of raising - as is a `PathLike` whose `__fspath__` returns bytes or raises, a path that cannot be canonicalized at all, a module whose `__file__` ACCESS raises (a lazy loader or proxy), and a value that cannot be formatted into the message itself), a missing or corrupt validation template, or a validator that crashes - the tool FAILS CLOSED: it writes nothing to stdout and exits non-zero with a message naming the actual cause, because emitting fragments whose one promise (they validate) could not be checked is the wrong direction exactly where something is already wrong. A caller who knowingly accepts unchecked output passes `--allow-unvalidated-output`, which emits them with a warning; that flag never suppresses a real validation failure. | `tests/test_chart_block.py` - render shape, aria derivation, JSON escaping, self-validation, `test_tools_dir_is_on_sys_path_for_self_validation`, invalid-id rejection, non-object spec rejection, CLI read-from-file, stdin, error-exit tests, and `ChartBlockUnvalidatedOutputTests` (unimportable validator naming its cause, a partial install missing the validator's own dependency, a foreign `validate` module, `test_a_non_string_module_file_is_refused_rather_than_raising`, `test_contained_refuses_a_non_path_value_without_raising`, `test_a_misbehaving_pathlike_file_is_refused_rather_than_raising`, `test_an_unformattable_module_file_still_comes_back_as_a_reason`, `test_a_path_that_cannot_be_canonicalized_is_refused_rather_than_raising`, `test_a_module_whose_file_attribute_raises_is_refused_rather_than_raising`, `test_a_result_that_cannot_be_repred_still_fails_closed_with_a_reason`, a missing template, a corrupt template with and without the opt-out, a crashing validator, the opt-out emitting with a warning, the opt-out NOT suppressing a real validation failure, and a working validator still validating under the flag) |

| CMH-ASCII-01 | The document producers rewrite AI "smart" typography - em/en dashes, an ellipsis glyph, curly single/double quotes, and non-breaking / zero-width spaces - to their plain-ASCII equivalents in visible PROSE, leaving VERBATIM regions (`<script>`, `<style>`, `<pre>`, inline `<code>`, HTML comments, CDATA, declarations/PIs) and tag markup (element names AND attribute values) untouched, so no report, plan, or deck ships off-style typography while machine data and code samples are never corrupted. `tools/authoring/normalize_typography.py` is the shared, idempotent, HTML-aware normalizer (a quote-aware `html.parser` tokenizer with a verbatim-element stack that fails closed on unterminated code and is linear on malformed input), with a `--check`/`--out`/in-place CLI and a plain-text `normalize_text()` helper for literal strings; `finalize.py` runs it by default (opt out `--no-normalize`) for reports/plans and `deck_scaffold.py` runs it by default on slide prose and on the plain-text `--label`/`--source`. Because the rewrite is length-changing it is a PRODUCER step meant to run BEFORE a document is first reviewed (normalizing prose after comments exist could shift their stored offsets), and it also flattens a legitimately intentional non-breaking space. | `tests/test_normalize_typography.py` - `NormalizeProseTests.test_replaces_each_ai_character_in_prose`, `VerbatimRegionsTests.test_pre_and_code_are_untouched`, `VerbatimRegionsTests.test_script_and_style_are_untouched`, `VerbatimRegionsTests.test_html_comment_is_untouched`, `HtmlAwarenessTests.test_quoted_gt_in_attribute_does_not_leak_into_prose`, `HtmlAwarenessTests.test_doctype_and_pi_with_quoted_gt_are_untouched`, `HtmlAwarenessTests.test_self_closing_verbatim_protects_tail`, `HtmlAwarenessTests.test_misnested_verbatim_stays_protected`, `HtmlAwarenessTests.test_many_unclosed_tags_are_linear`, `HtmlAwarenessTests.test_many_unmatched_end_tags_are_linear`, `NormalizeProseTests.test_idempotent`, `PlainTextTests.test_normalize_text_treats_input_as_literal`, `CliTests.test_check_reports_and_leaves_file`, `CliTests.test_in_place_rewrites`; `tests/test_finalize.py` - `test_normalizes_ai_typography_in_prose_by_default`, `test_no_normalize_flag_preserves_ai_typography`; `tests/test_deck_scaffold.py` - `test_scaffold_normalizes_ai_typography_in_slide_prose`, `test_scaffold_normalizes_ai_typography_in_label`, `test_no_normalize_flag_preserves_ai_typography` |
| CMH-TOOL-15 | `tools/authoring/retrofit.py` injects the full commentable layer into an existing unlayered HTML file, using parsed `<head>` and `<body>` insertion points, a CSS-only theme-vars block, a unique non-demo key, the content-root descriptor contract, optional `--root-selector "#id"`, matching NonShareable asset options, `--shareable`, and `--skip-selectors`; it accepts valid HTML5 optional-end-tag closures while rejecting malformed non-optional closures, keeps JS outside the CONTENT markers, avoids nested `<main>` by wrapping with `<div>` when the body already has a main element, refuses already-layered or ambiguous documents, self-closing non-void roots, and implicitly closed roots, ignores fake container tags inside comments or scripts, warns on likely CSS collisions, validates before writing, and leaves the target unchanged when validation fails. It also bakes the report/plan document-overview strip (CMH-STATS-01, opt out `--no-stats`) and de-dups an author-numbered ordered-list `.cm-toc` (CMH-TOC-10) before validating, and preserves the input file's DOMINANT newline (detected from the raw bytes) so a Windows-authored (CRLF) host is not silently normalized to LF on retrofit. Its staged write goes through the same shared mode handling as the upgrader (`_atomic_io.preserve_mode` / `quiet_remove`): retrofitting a `0644` host page in place keeps it world-readable instead of narrowing it to `mkstemp`'s `0600`, a `--out` to a path that does not exist yet takes the host page's mode (never wider than the process umask would create), and the destination is resolved with `realpath` so a symlinked target is rewritten rather than replaced - while `--copy-assets` companions still land beside the path the CALLER named, because bare-name refs resolve against the URL the document is opened by, not the inode it lives on. The stamped root's start tag is READ through the shared browser reading (`_browser_attrs.raw_attrs_pairs`, CMH-VAL-21) and REWRITTEN through the shared writer (`_browser_attrs.serialize_start_tag`), which matters more here than anywhere else because `--root-selector` points this tool at an ARBITRARY third-party document rather than at the skill's own artifact: the local `([^\s=/<>]+)` pattern it used before cannot even REPRESENT a name containing `=`, so the inert `=onclick` a browser sees (HTML5's unexpected-equals-sign-before-attribute-name state) was RENAMED into a live `onclick` handler, and the matching bare-name WRITE let two adjacent valueless attributes fuse into one carrying a value the host page never had (#1195). A valueless attribute is written back as `name=""`, which is the same attribute to a browser, and a value is decoded before it is re-escaped, so an authored `A &amp; B` round-trips instead of becoming the literal `A &amp;amp; B`. The tag NAME is split off HTML's way too (ASCII whitespace, `/` or `>`), not with Python's `str.split()`, which does not break on `/` and DOES break on NBSP and the vertical tab: `<div/id="content">` is a `div` carrying an `id` to a browser but was read as a tag NAMED `div/id="content"` with no attributes, so the stamp landed AFTER the host's own `id` and HTML's first-wins rule threw the stamped `id="commentRoot"` away, leaving the retrofitted document with no content root at all; `<div\u00a0onclick=alert(1) id="content">` is an unknown element carrying only `id`, but the Python split promoted its NBSP-joined tag-name text into a REAL `onclick`; and `<nav id=x/>` has the unquoted value `x/`, which the trailing-`/` strip truncated. `--skip-selectors` rewrites an arbitrary host element's start tag through the SAME reader and writer, so it carries the same guarantees plus one more: the source tag's self-closing ` /` is re-emitted, because dropping it un-closed a FOREIGN self-closing element and silently re-parented its next sibling. WHERE a start tag ENDS is decided by HTML's own tokenizer states too (`new_document._tag_end`): a `"`/`'` opens a quoted value only in the before-attribute-value state (right after the `=`), so an ordinary unquoted value carrying an apostrophe - `<div title=Bob's id="content">` - no longer makes the scan run past the real `>` and re-sync at the next quote in the document. That overshoot recorded an element extent covering LIVE host markup, and re-serializing the start tag then OVERWROTE it: a whole `<section>` of the host page was silently deleted, with a zero exit and a clean validation. | `tests/test_retrofit.py` - happy path and direct output contract, root-selector including empty and closed elements, shareable output, CSS-only theme block, implicit HTML5 closures, malformed non-optional closure rejection, existing-main wrapping, self-closing and implicitly closed root refusal, fake comment/script tags, demo-key and duplicate-root guards, already-layered refusal, malformed/ambiguous head/body, selector miss/multi-match, non-UTF-8 input, validation failure atomicity, skip-selectors, CSS collision warnings, strict validation, doc-stats default and opt-out, ordered-list TOC number de-dup, `test_preserves_crlf_line_endings`, `test_keeps_lf_when_lf_is_dominant_despite_a_stray_crlf`, `test_in_place_retrofit_preserves_the_host_file_mode`, `test_retrofit_out_to_a_new_file_takes_the_host_file_mode`, `test_retrofit_through_a_symlink_rewrites_the_real_file`, `test_root_selector_start_tag_round_trips_a_valueless_attribute`, `test_split_start_tag_terminates_the_name_the_way_html_does`, `test_root_selector_reads_the_tag_name_the_way_html_terminates_one`, `test_skip_selectors_round_trip_a_valueless_attribute_and_a_self_closing_tag`, `test_tag_end_only_treats_a_value_opening_quote_as_a_quote`, `test_a_quote_in_an_unquoted_value_does_not_swallow_host_content`, and `test_copy_assets_lands_beside_the_path_the_caller_named` tests |
| CMH-TOOL-HELP-01 | Every shipped CLI tool under `tools/<topic>/` (discovered recursively across the topic buckets; the `deck_common` shared library has no CLI and is excluded) answers `--help` and `-h` with exit 0 and prints usage text to stdout, so discovering a tool never treats the flag as a filename or fails. A tool that parses argv itself honors a `--` end-of-options separator: `-h`/`--help` is treated as a request for help only before a bare `--`, and any `-h`/`--help` after it is a positional data value (filename/query/id), not help. | `tests/test_cli_help.py` - `test_there_are_tools_to_check`, `test_every_tool_answers_help_with_exit_zero`, `test_help_flag_before_end_of_options_triggers_help`, `test_help_flag_after_end_of_options_is_data`, `test_dash_h_after_separator_is_treated_as_data_end_to_end` |

| CMH-TOOL-IMPORTS-01 | Guarded sibling-tool imports in the shipped tools are never SILENT (the #584 root-cause class was a deferred `import doc_stamp` swallowed by `except (OSError, ImportError): pass`). Across the WHOLE shipped tools tree: (1) every guarded sibling import RESOLVES in the shipped layout, so a missing/renamed sibling fails loudly here instead of silently degrading a feature at runtime; (2) any handler that would catch an import failure for a sibling-tool import - `except ImportError`/`ModuleNotFoundError`, or a broad `except Exception`/`BaseException`/bare except that would swallow one - makes the failure VISIBLE: it calls `_toolpath.warn_missing_tool`, unconditionally re-raises, or recovers by re-importing. Merely `pass`ing / assigning a sentinel / returning is forbidden (a later broad `except Exception` after an `except ImportError` that already consumed the import failure is exempt; the `_toolpath` bootstrap and stdlib imports are not sibling tools). The structural check enforces this for future code too. | `tests/test_tool_imports.py` - `test_no_shipped_import_fallback_is_silent`, `test_every_guarded_sibling_import_resolves_in_the_shipped_layout`, `test_warn_missing_tool_writes_a_visible_stderr_warning`, `test_a_module_level_fallback_warns_when_a_sibling_is_missing`; source: `tools/_toolpath.py` - `warn_missing_tool` |

## Claude Code compatibility

commentable-html output is a shareable HTML artifact that works with any agent, and the plugin is installable in Claude Code as well as the GitHub Copilot CLI. It ships a `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` beside the Copilot `plugin.json` and is listed in the repo-root `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (a subset mirror of `.github/plugin/marketplace.json`). The auto-updater is dual-agent too (its own `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and Claude `hooks/hooks.json`), so both published plugins install in either agent.

| Feature id | Behavior | Covering tests |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CMH-CLAUDE-01 | The plugin is installable in Claude Code: it ships `pkg/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` whose identity fields (name, version, description, author, license, keywords) mirror the Copilot `pkg/plugin.json`, declares `"skills": "./skills/"` which resolves to the shipped skill, and appears in the repo-root `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (whose entries mirror the matching `.github/plugin/marketplace.json` entries by name/version/source). `scripts/validate_claude_compat.py` checks this structurally and, when the `claude` CLI is present, runs `claude plugin validate --strict` on the marketplace and each Claude plugin dir. | `tests/test_claude_manifest.py` - `test_claude_plugin_manifest_ships`, `test_identity_fields_mirror_the_copilot_manifest`, `test_skills_path_resolves_and_skill_ships`; `scripts/test_validate_claude_compat.py` - `test_compatible_repo_has_no_errors`, the structural-failure tests, and `test_repo_manifests_are_claude_compatible` |
| CMH-HARNESS-01 | `dev/tools/skill_flow_harness.py` is a LOCAL-ONLY regression harness that drives the installed `copilot` executable non-interactively through the skill's create / retrofit / deck / export / validation-handoff routes (copying the shipped `pkg/skills/commentable-html` into `<ws>/.github/skills/` and extracting its `skill-resources.zip` so `copilot -C <ws> --add-dir <ws>` discovers the full shipped skill with file access SCOPED to the workspace, not `--allow-all-paths`) and then RE-VALIDATES each produced document READ-ONLY with the repo's own tools - `validate.py --strict --no-stamp`, `deck_validate.py --strict` for the deck, a self-contained (no companion asset) plus embedded-review-layer check for the shareable export, and on EVERY flow the AGENT-written `<meta name="commentable-html-validated">` stamp (the harness never writes it, so a skipped finalize/validate handoff is caught). A timeout is a hard FAIL; a completed non-zero exit with a strict-valid artifact is PASS with a surfaced note. It HARD-refuses to run under CI (in `main()` and again in `run_flow`), is wired into no workflow, clamps every path under the gitignored `tmp/skill-flow-harness/` (leading-separator / `..` / zip-slip / symlink / out-of-root `run_dir` escapes are rejected), and never lets one flow's error abort the batch. The flow corpus is `dev/tools/skill_flow_prompts.py`; usage and limitations are in `dev/tools/skill_flow_harness.md`. Because a live model run is non-hermetic and consumes credits, only the harness PLUMBING is automatically tested; the end-to-end model invocation is an intentional manual coverage gap. | `tests/test_skill_flow_harness.py` - `CiGuardTests`, `ScratchTests`, `CommandTests`, `ContentCheckTests`, `SafePathTests`, `WorkspaceTests`, `CorpusTests`, `PathTests` |
| CMH-BUILD-20 | `finalize.py` reads the document ONCE, threads it through the pure phase transforms in memory, and writes ONCE. Each phase used to re-read and re-write the whole file, so a 1.4 - 2.5 MB document paid a read/write pair and an independent full-document parse per phase (measured on a real document: 6 reads and 2 - 3 writes before validation and stamping read it again) - and `content_replace.py` calls finalize on every write-back, so the agent edit loop paid that on each iteration. The phase ORDER and every transform are unchanged, so the output is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the phase-by-phase pipeline. The per-phase `_run_*` read/modify/write wrappers are DELETED rather than kept: nothing called them once the pipeline threaded the document in memory, and leaving them would have let a future caller silently reintroduce the per-phase I/O this removes. Equivalence is proven against a FILE-CYCLE reference that reads, applies one phase, and writes back once per phase - the shape finalize had before this change - rather than by driving the same transforms in memory, which would compare the new code against itself; a guard test asserts the fixture exercises every always-on phase CUMULATIVELY (in the state the pipeline hands it), so equivalence can never be proven on a no-op. Byte equivalence alone CANNOT pin the phase order - every always-on phase commutes with its neighbours on any fixture tried, so a shuffled pipeline still emits identical bytes - so the order is pinned directly by recording the actual call sequence, and the opt-in phases (`--toc`, `--fix-skip`, `--inline-images`) get their own byte-equivalence run. Mutation checks confirmed the harness goes red both when a phase is dropped and when two adjacent phases are swapped (the swap slipped past the equivalence test alone). The win is proven structurally by counting the file operations EXACTLY (ONE read and ONE write for a changed document, including a strict-clean CLI run, and ZERO writes when nothing changed). Validation and the validated provenance stamp run on the same in-memory document - `validate.validate()` takes an optional `html=` and `validate.stamp_validated_text()` is the in-memory half of `_stamp_validated_file` - because otherwise a clean run still paid a second and third read to validate and stamp, plus a second write, not by a wall clock (a timing assertion would be flaky and the testing guidelines forbid it). Also fixes a quadratic scan in the KQL tokenizer: the per-identifier call test sliced the ENTIRE remaining query (`src[m.end():]`) on every token, and is now a bounded `[ \t]*\(` lookahead - `[ \t]`, not `\s`, so a newline before the paren is still not a call and the golden output is unchanged. | `tests/test_finalize_pipeline.py` - `PipelineEquivalenceTests` (`test_finalize_output_matches_the_phase_by_phase_pipeline`, `test_a_second_finalize_is_a_no_op`, `test_the_toc_phase_still_runs_when_requested`, `test_the_result_validates`), `PipelineEquivalenceTests.test_the_fixture_actually_exercises_every_phase`, `test_the_phases_run_in_the_documented_order`, `test_the_optional_phases_are_threaded_equivalently_too`, `IoAmplificationTests` (`test_the_pipeline_writes_the_document_once`, `test_the_pipeline_reads_the_document_once`, `test_a_strict_clean_run_still_writes_only_once`, `test_the_document_is_actually_stamped_when_clean`, `test_an_unchanged_document_is_not_rewritten`, `test_the_toc_run_does_not_add_a_read_write_pair`); the KQL golden output is pinned unchanged by `tests/test_kql_highlight.py` and `tests/test_highlight_parity.py`; source: `tools/authoring/finalize.py` - the `_apply_*` transforms and `finalize()`; `tools/kusto/kql_highlight.py` - `_CALL_AHEAD_RE` |
